tasks/AI_Powered_PPT\Q3_Review_Report.md:75:The first hit is load-bearing: the accent exists in a layout even though it is absent from the theme and master. The renderer contract has no branch for this state; it only describes theme-slot or master-hardcoded colour resolution (`src/aippt/render/renderer.py:143-146`). Inline code, callouts, and native diagrams therefore have no valid brand-colour source under the current manifest.
tasks/AI_Powered_PPT\Q3_Review_Report.md:77:Font extraction has the same scope problem. The catalog lists `맑은 고딕` and an empty East-Asian face (`reference/layout_catalog.md:11-15`), but package inspection found layout-level `뷰웍스 B` and `뷰웍스 M` typefaces. tasks/AI_Powered_PPT\Q3_Review_Report.md:87:Even for real text placeholders, `content_budget()` uses the full outer width and height (`src/aippt/analyze_pptx_master.py:312-365`) and does not subtract or model:
tasks/AI_Powered_PPT\Q3_Review_Report.md:108:- deck metadata says the renderer turns `deck.title` into a title slide (`src/aippt/ir/schema.json:16-33`);

tasks/AI_Powered_PPT\Q3_Review_Report.md:110:- code variants and overflow handling may emit continuation slides (`src/aippt/render/renderer.py:166-170`, `src/aippt/render/fit.py:136-150`).
tasks/AI_Powered_PPT\Q3_Review_Report.md:112:The HTML adapter also promises one card in -> one slide out (`src/aippt/adapters/html_adapter.py:98-105`). tasks/AI_Powered_PPT\Q3_Review_Report.md:120:The schema says blocks are assigned to non-title placeholders in order and then to free-floating shapes (`src/aippt/ir/schema.json:72-76`). That is ambiguous when:
tasks/AI_Powered_PPT\Q3_Review_Report.md:133:The corpus contains one ordered process with ten `.step-badge` items (`[REDACTED].html:350-359`), while `steps.items` is capped at six (`src/aippt/ir/schema.json:262-283`). The schema therefore rejects a construct it claims was derived from the corpus.
tasks/AI_Powered_PPT\Q3_Review_Report.md:151:`Deck.from_dict()` explicitly does not execute JSON Schema validation (`src/aippt/ir/models.py:467-494`). Its manual parsing accepts or mishandles several schema-invalid values: extra properties, invalid enums, excessive item counts, invalid lengths, empty run arrays, and some missing keys. `_parse_block()` also does not enforce all schema limits (`src/aippt/ir/models.py:342-418`). Depending on which adapter constructs a `Deck`, the “normative contract” can be bypassed.
tasks/AI_Powered_PPT\Q3_Review_Report.md:167:`image.source.value` has no maximum length or media validation; `path` is described as relative but is not constrained (`src/aippt/ir/schema.json:214-235`). This permits oversized base64 payloads and path traversal if a renderer resolves paths naively. Mermaid rendering similarly needs subprocess timeout, input-size limits, and a fixed working directory.
tasks/AI_Powered_PPT\Q3_Review_Report.md:177:The renderer contract says a `TABLE` placeholder cannot be filled directly and must be deleted after `shapes.add_table()` (`src/aippt/render/renderer.py:161-165`). python-pptx provides `TablePlaceholder.insert_table(rows, cols)`, which replaces the placeholder with a placeholder graphic frame and preserves its relationship/position semantics. The current template has no TABLE placeholder, but the generic renderer contract is still wrong. See the official [placeholder documentation](https://python-pptx.readthedocs.io/en/latest/user/placeholders-using.html) and [table documentation](https://python-pptx.readthedocs.io/en/latest/user/table.html).
tasks/AI_Powered_PPT\Q3_Review_Report.md:191:`_master_ph_for()` matches master placeholders by idx before type (`src/aippt/analyze_pptx_master.py:435-444`). python-pptx's layout-placeholder implementation maps layout types to a corresponding master placeholder type. An idx collision with a different type can therefore produce incorrect geometry/font inheritance in other templates. See the official [placeholder implementation](https://python-pptx.readthedocs.io/en/latest/_modules/pptx/shapes/placeholder.html).
tasks/AI_Powered_PPT\Q3_Review_Report.md:199:The diagram contract treats `begin_connect()` / `end_connect()` as straightforward (`src/aippt/render/diagrams.py:124-146`). The official [Connector API](https://python-pptx.readthedocs.io/en/latest/api/shapes.html) marks both methods experimental, reliable primarily for rectangular shapes, and warns that invalid connection-point indices can cause a load error.

tasks/AI_Powered_PPT\artifacts\codex-critic-stderr-2001-7815.txt:8494:+The first hit is load-bearing: the accent exists in a layout even though it is absent from the theme and master. The renderer contract has no branch for this state; it only describes theme-slot or master-hardcoded colour resolution (`src/aippt/render/renderer.py:143-146`). Inline code, callouts, and native diagrams therefore have no valid brand-colour source under the current manifest.
tasks/AI_Powered_PPT\artifacts\codex-critic-stderr-2001-7815.txt:8496:+Font extraction has the same scope problem. The catalog lists `맑은 고딕` and an empty East-Asian face (`reference/layout_catalog.md:11-15`), but package inspection found layout-level `뷰웍스 B` and `뷰웍스 M` typefaces. The theme also declares the Korean font through `<a:font script="Hang" typeface="맑은 고딕">`; `_typefaces()` reads only `latin`, `ea`, and `cs`, so it incorrectly treats the Korean mapping as absent (`src/aippt/analyze_pptx_master.py:136-144`).
tasks/AI_Powered_PPT\artifacts\codex-critic-stderr-2001-7815.txt:8506:+Even for real text placeholders, `content_budget()` uses the full outer width and height (`src/aippt/analyze_pptx_master.py:312-365`) and does not subtract or model:
tasks/AI_Powered_PPT\artifacts\codex-critic-stderr-2001-7815.txt:8527:+- deck metadata says the renderer turns `deck.title` into a title slide (`src/aippt/ir/schema.json:16-33`);
tasks/AI_Powered_PPT\artifacts\codex-critic-stderr-2001-7815.txt:8528:+- renderer output must have exactly `len(ir.slides)` slides (`src/aippt/render/renderer.py:76-80`);
tasks/AI_Powered_PPT\artifacts\codex-critic-stderr-2001-7815.txt:8529:+- code variants and overflow handling may emit continuation slides (`src/aippt/render/renderer.py:166-170`, `src/aippt/render/fit.py:136-150`).
tasks/AI_Powered_PPT\artifacts\codex-critic-stderr-2001-7815.txt:8531:+The HTML adapter also promises one card in -> one slide out (`src/aippt/adapters/html_adapter.py:98-105`). An implicit title slide or renderer-created continuation breaks slide count, stable IDs, source mapping, visual-QA mapping, and deterministic re-runs.
tasks/AI_Powered_PPT\artifacts\codex-critic-stderr-2001-7815.txt:8539:+The schema says blocks are assigned to non-title placeholders in order and then to free-floating shapes (`src/aippt/ir/schema.json:72-76`). That is ambiguous when:
tasks/AI_Powered_PPT\artifacts\codex-critic-stderr-2001-7815.txt:8552:+The corpus contains one ordered process with ten `.step-badge` items (`[REDACTED].html:350-359`), while `steps.items` is capped at six (`src/aippt/ir/schema.json:262-283`). The schema therefore rejects a construct it claims was derived from the corpus.
tasks/AI_Powered_PPT\artifacts\codex-critic-stderr-2001-7815.txt:8570:+`Deck.from_dict()` explicitly does not execute JSON Schema validation (`src/aippt/ir/models.py:467-494`). Its manual parsing accepts or mishandles several schema-invalid values: extra properties, invalid enums, excessive item counts, invalid lengths, empty run arrays, and some missing keys. `_parse_block()` also does not enforce all schema limits (`src/aippt/ir/models.py:342-418`). Depending on which adapter constructs a `Deck`, the “normative contract” can be bypassed.
tasks/AI_Powered_PPT\artifacts\codex-critic-stderr-2001-7815.txt:8586:+`image.source.value` has no maximum length or media validation; `path` is described as relative but is not constrained (`src/aippt/ir/schema.json:214-235`). This permits oversized base64 payloads and path traversal if a renderer resolves paths naively. Mermaid rendering similarly needs subprocess timeout, input-size limits, and a fixed working directory.
tasks/AI_Powered_PPT\artifacts\codex-critic-stderr-2001-7815.txt:8596:+The renderer contract says a `TABLE` placeholder cannot be filled directly and must be deleted after `shapes.add_table()` (`src/aippt/render/renderer.py:161-165`). python-pptx provides `TablePlaceholder.insert_table(rows, cols)`, which replaces the placeholder with a placeholder graphic frame and preserves its relationship/position semantics. The current template has no TABLE placeholder, but the generic renderer contract is still wrong. See the official [placeholder documentation](https://python-pptx.readthedocs.io/en/latest/user/placeholders-using.html) and [table documentation](https://python-pptx.readthedocs.io/en/latest/user/table.html).
tasks/AI_Powered_PPT\artifacts\codex-critic-stderr-2001-7815.txt:8610:+`_master_ph_for()` matches master placeholders by idx before type (`src/aippt/analyze_pptx_master.py:435-444`). python-pptx's layout-placeholder implementation maps layout types to a corresponding master placeholder type. An idx collision with a different type can therefore produce incorrect geometry/font inheritance in other templates. See the official [placeholder implementation](https://python-pptx.readthedocs.io/en/latest/_modules/pptx/shapes/placeholder.html).
tasks/AI_Powered_PPT\artifacts\codex-critic-stderr-2001-7815.txt:8618:+The diagram contract treats `begin_connect()` / `end_connect()` as straightforward (`src/aippt/render/diagrams.py:124-146`). The official [Connector API](https://python-pptx.readthedocs.io/en/latest/api/shapes.html) marks both methods experimental, reliable primarily for rectangular shapes, and warns that invalid connection-point indices can cause a load error.

Inline code, callouts, and native diagrams therefore have no valid brand-colour source under the current manifest.
tasks/AI_Powered_PPT\artifacts\codex-critic-stderr-2001-7815.txt:9086:+Font extraction has the same scope problem. The catalog lists `맑은 고딕` and an empty East-Asian face (`reference/layout_catalog.md:11-15`), but package inspection found layout-level `뷰웍스 B` and `뷰웍스 M` typefaces. The theme also declares the Korean font through `<a:font script="Hang" typeface="맑은 고딕">`; `_typefaces()` reads only `latin`, `ea`, and `cs`, so it incorrectly treats the Korean mapping as absent (`src/aippt/analyze_pptx_master.py:136-144`).
tasks/AI_Powered_PPT\artifacts\codex-critic-stderr-2001-7815.txt:9096:+Even for real text placeholders, `content_budget()` uses the full outer width and height (`src/aippt/analyze_pptx_master.py:312-365`) and does not subtract or model:

tasks/AI_Powered_PPT\artifacts\codex-critic-stderr-2001-7815.txt:9118:+- renderer output must have exactly `len(ir.slides)` slides (`src/aippt/render/renderer.py:76-80`);
tasks/AI_Powered_PPT\artifacts\codex-critic-stderr-2001-7815.txt:9119:+- code variants and overflow handling may emit continuation slides (`src/aippt/render/renderer.py:166-170`, `src/aippt/render/fit.py:136-150`).
tasks/AI_Powered_PPT\artifacts\codex-critic-stderr-2001-7815.txt:9121:+The HTML adapter also promises one card in -> one slide out (`src/aippt/adapters/html_adapter.py:98-105`). An implicit title slide or renderer-created continuation breaks slide count, stable IDs, source mapping, visual-QA mapping, and deterministic re-runs.
tasks/AI_Powered_PPT\artifacts\codex-critic-stderr-2001-7815.txt:9129:+The schema says blocks are assigned to non-title placeholders in order and then to free-floating shapes (`src/aippt/ir/schema.json:72-76`). That is ambiguous when:
tasks/AI_Powered_PPT\artifacts\codex-critic-stderr-2001-7815.txt:9142:+The corpus contains one ordered process with ten `.step-badge` items (`[REDACTED].html:350-359`), while `steps.items` is capped at six (`src/aippt/ir/schema.json:262-283`). The schema therefore rejects a construct it claims was derived from the corpus.
tasks/AI_Powered_PPT\artifacts\codex-critic-stderr-2001-7815.txt:9160:+`Deck.from_dict()` explicitly does not execute JSON Schema validation (`src/aippt/ir/models.py:467-494`). Its manual parsing accepts or mishandles several schema-invalid values: extra properties, invalid enums, excessive item counts, invalid lengths, empty run arrays, and some missing keys. `_parse_block()` also does not enforce all schema limits (`src/aippt/ir/models.py:342-418`). Depending on which adapter constructs a `Deck`, the “normative contract” can be bypassed.
tasks/AI_Powered_PPT\artifacts\codex-critic-stderr-2001-7815.txt:9176:+`image.source.value` has no maximum length or media validation; `path` is described as relative but is not constrained (`src/aippt/ir/schema.json:214-235`). This permits oversized base64 payloads and path traversal if a renderer resolves paths naively. Mermaid rendering similarly needs subprocess timeout, input-size limits, and a fixed working directory.
tasks/AI_Powered_PPT\artifacts\codex-critic-stderr-2001-7815.txt:9186:+The renderer contract says a `TABLE` placeholder cannot be filled directly and must be deleted after `shapes.add_table()` (`src/aippt/render/renderer.py:161-165`). python-pptx provides `TablePlaceholder.insert_table(rows, cols)`, which replaces the placeholder with a placeholder graphic frame and preserves its relationship/position semantics. The current template has no TABLE placeholder, but the generic renderer contract is still wrong. See the official [placeholder documentation](https://python-pptx.readthedocs.io/en/latest/user/placeholders-using.html) and [table documentation](https://python-pptx.readthedocs.io/en/latest/user/table.html).
tasks/AI_Powered_PPT\artifacts\codex-critic-stderr-2001-7815.txt:9200:+`_master_ph_for()` matches master placeholders by idx before type (`src/aippt/analyze_pptx_master.py:435-444`). python-pptx's layout-placeholder implementation maps layout types to a corresponding master placeholder type. An idx collision with a different type can therefore produce incorrect geometry/font inheritance in other templates. See the official [placeholder implementation](https://python-pptx.readthedocs.io/en/latest/_modules/pptx/shapes/placeholder.html).
tasks/AI_Powered_PPT\artifacts\codex-critic-stderr-2001-7815.txt:9208:+The diagram contract treats `begin_connect()` / `end_connect()` as straightforward (`src/aippt/render/diagrams.py:124-146`). The official [Connector API](https://python-pptx.readthedocs.io/en/latest/api/shapes.html) marks both methods experimental, reliable primarily for rectangular shapes, and warns that invalid connection-point indices can cause a load error.

tasks/AI_Powered_PPT\artifacts\codex-critic-stderr-6619-8346.txt:1135:`Deck.from_dict()` explicitly does not execute JSON Schema validation (`src/aippt/ir/models.py:467-494`). Its manual parsing accepts or mishandles several schema-invalid values: extra properties, invalid enums, excessive item counts, invalid lengths, empty run arrays, and some missing keys. `_parse_block()` also does not enforce all schema limits (`src/aippt/ir/models.py:342-418`). Depending on which adapter constructs a `Deck`, the “normative contract” can be bypassed.
tasks/AI_Powered_PPT\artifacts\codex-critic-stderr-6619-8346.txt:1151:`image.source.value` has no maximum length or media validation; `path` is described as relative but is not constrained (`src/aippt/ir/schema.json:214-235`). This permits oversized base64 payloads and path traversal if a renderer resolves paths naively. Mermaid rendering similarly needs subprocess timeout, input-size limits, and a fixed working directory.
tasks/AI_Powered_PPT\artifacts\codex-critic-stderr-6619-8346.txt:1161:The renderer contract says a `TABLE` placeholder cannot be filled directly and must be deleted after `shapes.add_table()` (`src/aippt/render/renderer.py:161-165`). python-pptx provides `TablePlaceholder.insert_table(rows, cols)`, which replaces the placeholder with a placeholder graphic frame and preserves its relationship/position semantics. The current template has no TABLE placeholder, but the generic renderer contract is still wrong. See the official [placeholder documentation](https://python-pptx.readthedocs.io/en/latest/user/placeholders-using.html) and [table documentation](https://python-pptx.readthedocs.io/en/latest/user/table.html).
tasks/AI_Powered_PPT\artifacts\codex-critic-stderr-6619-8346.txt:1175:`_master_ph_for()` matches master placeholders by idx before type (`src/aippt/analyze_pptx_master.py:435-444`). python-pptx's layout-placeholder implementation maps layout types to a corresponding master placeholder type. An idx collision with a different type can therefore produce incorrect geometry/font inheritance in other templates. See the official [placeholder implementation](https://python-pptx.readthedocs.io/en/latest/_modules/pptx/shapes/placeholder.html).
tasks/AI_Powered_PPT\artifacts\codex-critic-stderr-6619-8346.txt:1183:The diagram contract treats `begin_connect()` / `end_connect()` as straightforward (`src/aippt/render/diagrams.py:124-146`). The official [Connector API](https://python-pptx.readthedocs.io/en/latest/api/shapes.html) marks both methods experimental, reliable primarily for rectangular shapes, and warns that invalid connection-point indices can cause a load error.

  958:   // Strip C0 EXCEPT \t and \n. validate.py rejects them; stripping here means the user never gets a
  959:   // 422 for a character they cannot see. (혻 is NOT C0 and is preserved verbatim.)
  960:   function stripC0(t) { return t.replace(/[\x00-\x08\x0b-\x1f]/g, ''); }
  961: 
  962:   var BLOCK_TAGS = { DIV: 1, P: 1, LI: 1, H1: 1, H2: 1, H3: 1, H4: 1, H5: 1, H6: 1, BLOCKQUOTE: 1, PRE: 1 };
  963: 
  964:   function hex2(n) { return ('0' + n.toString(16)).slice(-2).toUpperCase(); }
  965: 
  966:   function cssColorToHex(raw) {
  967:     var v = String(raw).trim();
  968:     var m = v.match(/^rgba?\(\s*(\d+)\s*,\s*(\d+)\s*,\s*(\d+)/i);
  969:     if (m) return '#' + hex2(+m[1]) + hex2(+m[2]) + hex2(+m[3]);
  970:     m = v.match(/^#([0-9a-f]{6})$/i);
  971:     if (m) return '#' + m[1].toUpperCase();
  972:     m = v.match(/^#([0-9a-f]{3})$/i);
  973:     if (m) {
  974:       var s = m[1];
  975:       return '#' + (s[0] + s[0] + s[1] + s[1] + s[2] + s[2]).toUpperCase();
  976:     }
  977:     return null; // a named colour or anything exotic: fall back to the element default
  978:   }

  980:   // The INLINE style attribute only -- never the browser's COMPUTED style, which is banned (Gate 1)
  981:   // and would silently bake the CSS default colour into every single run.
  982:   function colorOf(node) {
  983:     var inline = node.style && node.style.color;
  984:     if (inline) { var c = cssColorToHex(inline); if (c) return c; }
  985:     if (node.tagName === 'FONT') {
  986:       var attr = node.getAttribute('color');
  987:       if (attr) { var f = cssColorToHex(attr); if (f) return f; }
  988:     }
  989:     return null;
  990:   }

  992:   /* normalizeText(root) -> [paragraph, ...] | null
  993:    *
  994:    * Every run carries t, size and color -- all three are REQUIRED by the schema. An un-coloured run
  995:    * inherits fontRef -> lt1, which is WHITE, and that is the rule that stops white-on-white text.
  996:    *
  997:    * defBold/defItalic are the ELEMENT's flags, and they are OR-ed into every run -- the same
  998:    * relationship defSize and defColor already have with the runs, just spelled out. The IR has no
  999:    * element-level bold to serialize them into; run.bold is the only place they can land. A <b> or a
 1000:    * <strong> INSIDE the body (a paste, or the browser's own Ctrl+B) still bolds its own run on its
 1001:    * own, which is why turning the element flag off has to remove those tags -- see setTextFlag().
 1002:    */
 1003:   function normalizeText(root, defSize, defColor, defBold, defItalic) {
 1004:     var paras = [];
 1005:     var cur = null;
 1006: 
 1007:     function startPara() { cur = []; paras.push(cur); }
 1008:     startPara();
 1009: 
 1010:     function push(text, fmt) {
 1011:       if (!text) return;
 1012:       var run = { t: text, size: defSize, color: fmt.color || defColor };
 1013:       if (fmt.bold || defBold) run.bold = true;
 1014:       if (fmt.italic || defItalic) run.italic = true;
 1015:       var last = cur[cur.length - 1];
 1016:       // Merge adjacent runs with identical formatting.
 1017:       if (last && !!last.bold === !!run.bold && !!last.italic === !!run.italic && last.color === run.color) {
 1018:         last.t += run.t;
 1019:       } else {
 1020:         cur.push(run);
 1021:       }
 1022:     }
 1023: 
 1024:     function walk(node, fmt) {
 1025:       var kids = node.childNodes;
 1026:       for (var i = 0; i < kids.length; i++) {
 1027:         var child = kids[i];
 1028: 
 1029:         if (child.nodeType === 3) { // text
 1030:           push(stripC0(child.nodeValue), fmt);
 1031:           continue;
 1032:         }
 1033:         if (child.nodeType !== 1) continue; // comments and the rest
 1034: 
 1035:         var tag = child.tagName;
 1036: 
 1037:         if (tag === 'BR') {
 1038:           // A <br> that is the LAST child of its block is the browser's own filler -- Chrome puts
 1039:           // one in every empty contenteditable block to give it height. Emitting it would add a
 1040:           // phantom trailing line to the PPTX.
 1041:           if (child === node.lastChild) continue;
 1042:           push('\n', fmt); // style.py splits on \n and emits <a:br/>
 1043:           continue;
 1044:         }
 1045: 
 1046:         var f = { bold: fmt.bold, italic: fmt.italic, color: fmt.color };
 1047:         if (tag === 'B' || tag === 'STRONG') f.bold = true;
 1048:         if (tag === 'I' || tag === 'EM') f.italic = true;
 1049:         var c = colorOf(child);
 1050:         if (c) f.color = c;
 1051: 
 1052:         if (BLOCK_TAGS[tag]) startPara(); // a block element is a NEW PARAGRAPH
 1053:         walk(child, f);
 1054:       }
 1055:     }
 1056: 
 1057:     walk(root, { bold: false, italic: false, color: null });
 1058: 
 1059:     // Drop empty runs, then empty paragraphs -- browsers leave trailing empty <div>s behind
 1060:     // constantly.
 1061:     var out = [];
 1062:     for (var p = 0; p < paras.length; p++) {
 1063:       var runs = paras[p].filter(function (r) { return r.t !== ''; });
 1064:       if (runs.length) out.push({ runs: runs });
 1065:     }
 1066:     // If nothing survives, the caller emits "text": null -- NOT paragraphs: [], which the schema
 1067:     // rejects (minItems: 1).
 1068:     return out.length ? out : null;
 1069:   }
 1070: 
 1071:   // The title is the same walk, flattened to a plain STRING. A "\n" becomes an <a:br/> in
 1072:   // placeholder 10.
 1073:   function titleString() {
 1074:     var out = '';
 1075:     (function walk(node) {
 1076:       var kids = node.childNodes;
 1077:       for (var i = 0; i < kids.length; i++) {
 1078:         var child = kids[i];
 1079:         if (child.nodeType === 3) { out += child.nodeValue; continue; }
 1080:         if (child.nodeType !== 1) continue;
 1081:         if (child.tagName === 'BR') {
 1082:           if (child !== node.lastChild) out += '\n';
 1083:           continue;
 1084:         }
 1085:         if (BLOCK_TAGS[child.tagName] && out !== '') out += '\n';
 1086:         walk(child);
 1087:       }
 1088:     })(titleField);
 1089:     return stripC0(out).replace(/\n+$/, '');
 1090:   }

 1092:   /* [REDACTED] serializer */

 1094:   function toIR(el) {
 1095:     var s = read(el);
 1096: 
 1097:     if (s.type === 'connector') {
 1098:       // No w/h. No text. No z. Exactly the schema's keys for this type and NOTHING else --
 1099:       // additionalProperties: false means one leaked data-* is a hard rejection.
 1100:       return {
 1101:         id: s.id, type: 'connector', kind: s.kind,
 1102:         x1: round6(s.x1), y1: round6(s.y1), x2: round6(s.x2), y2: round6(s.y2),
 1103:         color: s.color, width: round6(s.width), arrow: s.arrow,
 1104:       };
 1105:     }
 1106: 
 1107:     var out = {
 1108:       id: s.id, type: s.type,
 1109:       x: round6(s.x), y: round6(s.y), w: round6(s.w), h: round6(s.h),
 1110:       fill: s.fill ? s.fill : null, // REQUIRED even when null
 1111:     };
 1112:     if (s.rot) out.rotation = round6(s.rot);
 1113:     if (s.strokeColor) out.stroke = { color: s.strokeColor, width: round6(s.strokeWidth) };
 1114: 
 1115:     var body = el.querySelector('.txt .body');
 1116:     var paragraphs = body ? normalizeText(body, s.size, s.color, s.bold, s.italic) : null;
 1117:     out.text = paragraphs ? { align: s.align, valign: s.valign, paragraphs: paragraphs } : null;
 1118:     return out;
 1119:   }

 1126:         template: 'reference/renderer_template.pptx', // informational ONLY; the server ignores it

 1133:       elements: [].slice.call(stage.querySelectorAll(':scope > .el')).map(toIR),
 1134:     };

 1282:   /* [REDACTED] run.bold / run.italic
 1283:    *
 1284:    * The two IR properties the Phase 1B panel never exposed. They are ELEMENT-level controls over a
 1285:    * PER-RUN property, and the asymmetry is the whole difficulty:
 1286:    *
 1287:    *   ON  -- set the flag. normalizeText() OR-s it into every run on the way out, and style.css
 1288:    *          shows it via [data-bold='1']. Nothing is written into the body.
 1289:    *   OFF -- clear the flag AND unwrap every <b>/<strong> (resp. <i>/<em>) inside the body.
 1290:    *
 1291:    * The unwrap is not tidying, it is the difference between working and not. run.bold comes from
 1292:    * those tags as well as from the flag -- paragraphsToHTML() writes them on import, and the
 1293:    * browser's own Ctrl+B and any paste will too -- so an "off" that merely stopped ADDING bold
 1294:    * would leave the text bold on screen and in the .pptx, with the button showing un-pressed. The
 1295:    * control would look broken because it would BE broken.
 1296:    *
 1297:    * The cost is honest and worth stating: turning bold off on an element whose runs were only
 1298:    * PARTLY bold flattens that distinction. "Make this element not bold" is what the button says, and
 1299:    * it is what the button does.
 1300:    */
 1301:   function unwrapTags(root, tags) {
 1302:     var node;
 1303:     // A DOM edit, not a DOM measurement. normalizeText() already reads this very subtree -- it is
 1304:     // where runs come from -- so the body is state, not geometry, and the Prime Directive is silent
 1305:     // on it. Nested tags terminate: the outermost match is removed each pass.
 1306:     while ((node = root.querySelector(tags.join(',')))) {
 1307:       var parent = node.parentNode;
 1308:       while (node.firstChild) parent.insertBefore(node.firstChild, node);
 1309:       parent.removeChild(node);
 1310:     }
 1311:     root.normalize(); // merge the text nodes the unwrap left adjacent
 1312:   }
 1313: 
 1314:   function setTextFlag(which, on) {
 1315:     if (!selected || isConnector()) return; // a connector carries no text at all (schema: no `text`)
 1316:     beginTx(); // the unwrap and the flag are ONE undo step, not two
 1317:     var body = selected.querySelector('.txt .body');
 1318:     if (!on && body) unwrapTags(body, which === 'bold' ? ['b', 'strong'] : ['i', 'em']);
 1319:     var patch = {};
 1320:     patch[which] = on;
 1321:     patchSelected(patch); // geometry is untouched, so the judge cannot refuse this
 1322:     endTx();
 1323:     syncProps();
 1324:   }

 1344:   // of its array. So they are undoable for free, and pushing BEFORE the move is the whole of it.
 1345:   $('b-raise').addEventListener('click', function () {

 1348:     while (next && !next.classList.contains('el')) next = next.nextElementSibling;

 1355:     if (!selected) return;

 1357:     while (prev && !prev.classList.contains('el')) prev = prev.previousElementSibling;
 1358:     if (!prev) return;

 1368:   function deleteSelected() {
 1369:     if (!selected) return;
 1370:     // BEFORE the removal. The snapshot IS the thing we go back to -- taken afterwards it would
 1371:     // record the stage without the element, and Delete would become permanent with extra steps.
 1372:     pushUndo(snapshot());
 1373:     var el = selected;
 1374:     select(null);
 1375:     el.remove();
 1376:     setStatus('Deleted. (Ctrl+Z restores it.)');
 1377:   }

 1379:   function duplicateSelected() {
 1380:     if (!selected) return;
 1381:     var s = read(selected);
 1382:     s.id = nextId(); // a NEW id -- validate.py rejects duplicates
 1383:     var OFFSET = 8;
 1384:     if (s.type === 'connector') {
 1385:       s.x1 += OFFSET; s.y1 += OFFSET; s.x2 += OFFSET; s.y2 += OFFSET;
 1386:     } else {
 1387:       s.x += OFFSET; s.y += OFFSET;
 1388:     }
 1389:     var legal = applyConstraints(s, { alt: true });
 1390:     if (!legal) { flashBlocked(selected); setStatus('The copy would not fit legally.'); return; }
 1391: 
 1392:     // The judge has spoken and the copy WILL land, so only NOW is this a step. Pushing before the
 1393:     // legality check would leave a no-op undo behind every refused duplicate.
 1394:     pushUndo(snapshot());
 1395:     var copy = addEl(makeEl(legal));
 1396:     var srcBody = selected.querySelector('.txt .body');
 1397:     var dstBody = copy.querySelector('.txt .body');
 1398:     if (srcBody && dstBody) dstBody.innerHTML = srcBody.innerHTML;
 1399:     select(copy);
 1400:     setStatus('Duplicated as ' + legal.id + '.');
 1401:   }
 1402: 
 1403:   /* Reflect the selection into the property panel -- and, now, decide whether there IS one.
 1404:    *
 1405:    * The panel is WRITTEN FROM the element's dataset and read back only as an edit. It holds no
 1406:    * state of its own, which is why showing and hiding it is safe: there is nothing in it to lose.
 1407:    *
 1408:    * It is hidden with nothing selected because a panel of controls that patch nothing is a panel
 1409:    * that lies -- every one of these inputs was, in Phase 1B, live and inert at the same time.
 1410:    */
 1411:   function syncProps() {
 1412:     var s = selected ? read(selected) : null;
 1413:     props.hidden = !s;
 1414:     if (!s) { setStatus('Ready.'); return; }
 1415: 
 1416:     var isConn = s.type === 'connector';
 1417: 
 1418:     /* Show only the controls this type actually HAS. The IR gives a connector no fill, no text and
 1419:      * no rotation -- offering them would be offering a property that cannot be serialized, and the
 1420:      * JS guards (`if (isConnector()) return`) would silently eat the edit. A control that does
 1421:      * nothing when you use it is worse than one that is not there. */
 1422:     var scoped = props.querySelectorAll('[data-for]');
 1423:     for (var i = 0; i < scoped.length; i++) {
 1424:       scoped[i].hidden = (scoped[i].getAttribute('data-for') === 'connector') !== isConn;
 1425:     }
 1426: 
 1427:     $('p-what').textContent = s.type;
 1428:     $('p-which').textContent = s.id;

 1453:   function editingText(t) {
 1454:     return t && (t.isContentEditable || t.tagName === 'INPUT' || t.tagName === 'SELECT' || t.tagName === 'TEXTAREA');

 1457:   window.addEventListener('keydown', function (ev) {
 1458:     /* UNDO/REDO FIRST, and deliberately in FRONT of the editingText() guard.
 1459:      *
 1460:      * The property panel is made of <input>s, so an undo that stopped working while the focus was
 1461:      * still in the size box -- which is exactly where the focus IS, right after the edit you want to
 1462:      * undo -- would be an undo nobody trusts.
 1463:      *
 1464:      * The ONE exception is a live contenteditable. Inside the text, the browser's own undo is
 1465:      * per-keystroke and is what a user expects Ctrl+Z to do there; ours is per-session and would
 1466:      * throw away the whole paragraph. So we stand back and let it run. The snapshot taken when the
 1467:      * caret went in still makes the edit a single step from OUTSIDE, once the text is closed, which
 1468:      * is the granularity the stage wants anyway.
 1469:      */

 1472:         !(ev.target && ev.target.isContentEditable)) {
 1473:       ev.preventDefault();
 1474:       if (k === 'y' || ev.shiftKey) redo(); else undo(); // Ctrl+Y and Ctrl+Shift+Z both redo
 1475:       return;
 1476:     }

 1478:     if (editingText(ev.target)) return;

 1481:       if (selected) { ev.preventDefault(); deleteSelected(); }

 1485:       ev.preventDefault();
 1486:       duplicateSelected();
 1487:       return;
 1488:     }
 1489:     if (ev.key === 'Escape') select(null);
 1490:   });

  375:   function read(el) {
  376:     var d = el.dataset;
  377:     var s = { id: d.id, type: d.type };
  378:     if (d.type === 'connector') {
  379:       s.x1 = +d.x1; s.y1 = +d.y1; s.x2 = +d.x2; s.y2 = +d.y2;
  380:       s.kind = d.kind; s.arrow = d.arrow; s.color = d.color; s.width = +d.width;
  381:     } else {
  382:       s.x = +d.x; s.y = +d.y; s.w = +d.w; s.h = +d.h; s.rot = +d.rot || 0;
  383:       s.fill = d.fill || '';
  384:       s.strokeColor = d.strokeColor || '';
  385:       s.strokeWidth = d.strokeWidth === undefined || d.strokeWidth === '' ? 1 : +d.strokeWidth;
  386:       s.size = +d.size; s.color = d.color; s.align = d.align; s.valign = d.valign;
  387:       // A dataset value is a STRING, so '' and '0' and 'false' are all truthy traps. Compare.
  388:       s.bold = d.bold === '1'; s.italic = d.italic === '1';
  389:     }
  390:     return s;

  393:   function write(el, s) {
  394:     var d = el.dataset;
  395:     d.id = s.id; d.type = s.type;
  396:     if (s.type === 'connector') {
  397:       d.x1 = s.x1; d.y1 = s.y1; d.x2 = s.x2; d.y2 = s.y2;
  398:       d.kind = s.kind; d.arrow = s.arrow; d.color = s.color; d.width = s.width;
  399:     } else {
  400:       d.x = s.x; d.y = s.y; d.w = s.w; d.h = s.h; d.rot = s.rot || 0;
  401:       d.fill = s.fill; d.strokeColor = s.strokeColor; d.strokeWidth = s.strokeWidth;
  402:       d.size = s.size; d.color = s.color; d.align = s.align; d.valign = s.valign;
  403:       // '1' or '' -- style.css keys off [data-bold='1'], so "off" must not be the string 'false'.
  404:       d.bold = s.bold ? '1' : ''; d.italic = s.italic ? '1' : '';

  406:   }
  407: 
  408:   /* commit -- the ONE way state changes. Nothing writes dataset geometry except through here.
  409:    *
  410:    * Which is exactly why undo hangs off it: "every mutation is undoable" is only a checkable
  411:    * sentence because there is one door to hang the check on.
  412:    */
  413:   function commit(el, patch, opt) {
  414:     var cur = read(el);
  415:     for (var k in patch) cur[k] = patch[k];
  416:     var legal = applyConstraints(cur, opt);
  417:     if (!legal) { flashBlocked(el); return false; }
  418:     // BEFORE the write, and only when we already know the write will happen: a REFUSED frame must
  419:     // not cost an undo step, or dragging a box into the header would fill the stack with states the
  420:     // user cannot see the difference between. Inside a gesture the snapshot was taken once already,
  421:     // at beginTx() -- taking another one every pointermove frame is the one place this could get
  422:     // expensive.
  423:     var before = txOpen ? null : snapshot();
  424:     write(el, legal);
  425:     renderEl(el);
  426:     noteChange(before);
  427:     return true;
  428:   }
  429: 
  430:   function flashBlocked(el) {
  431:     // Restart the animation across a frame boundary rather than by poking a layout property --
  432:     // reading one back would measure the DOM, which is the whole thing we do not do.
  433:     el.classList.remove('blocked');
  434:     requestAnimationFrame(function () { el.classList.add('blocked'); });
  435:     setTimeout(function () { el.classList.remove('blocked'); }, 400);
  436:   }
  437: 
  438:   /* ================================================================================ undo / redo
  439:    *
  440:    * SNAPSHOT-BASED, and that is a decision, not a shortcut.
  441:    *
  442:    * The obvious alternative -- a stack of commands, each with an inverse -- needs an inverse for
  443:    * every mutation the editor has. Move and resize invert easily. Raise/lower reorder the DOM.
  444:    * Delete has to remember where in the z-order the element WAS. Load replaces the entire stage.
  445:    * Duplicate mints an id. Each of those is a separate inverse, and each inverse that is subtly
  446:    * wrong is a silent corruption that surfaces three undos later, somewhere else. A snapshot has
  447:    * exactly ONE inverse and every mutation shares it.
  448:    *
  449:    * A snapshot is JS STATE ONLY -- dataset (via read()), the contenteditable's innerHTML, and DOM
  450:    * ORDER, which IS the z-order. It measures nothing, and restoring one rebuilds the elements
  451:    * through makeEl(), i.e. back down the same one-way state -> CSS path every other write takes.
  452:    * Undo therefore cannot reintroduce a device pixel, which is the one way an undo stack could
  453:    * plausibly have broken the Prime Directive.
  454:    *
  455:    * WHAT IS NOT IN A SNAPSHOT: zoom and the current tool. They are VIEW, not document. Undoing a
  456:    * move and having the zoom jump under you is not undo, it is time travel.
  457:    *
  458:    * GESTURE GROUPING. A drag commits a frame per pointermove, so ungrouped, one drag across the
  459:    * stage would be a hundred undo steps and Ctrl+Z would be useless. beginTx() at the gesture's
  460:    * start takes ONE snapshot; the first successful commit inside the gesture pushes it; endTx() at
  461:    * pointerup throws it away if nothing was ever committed -- so a click that merely SELECTS costs
  462:    * no history at all, which is the case that would otherwise make the stack unreadable.
  463:    */
  464: 
  465:   var HIST_MAX = 100; // deep enough to be an undo stack, bounded so a long session cannot grow forever
  466: 
  467:   var past = [];
  468:   var future = [];
  469: 
  470:   var txOpen = false;   // a gesture is in flight
  471:   var txBefore = null;  // the stage as it was when that gesture began
  472:   var txPushed = false; // ...and whether that snapshot is already on the stack
  473: 
  474:   function snapshot() {
  475:     return {
  476:       title: titleField.innerHTML,
  477:       // :scope > .el -- the same query the serializer uses, so the snapshot sees exactly what the
  478:       // IR sees: no pseudo-element chrome, no title field, and the array order IS the z-order.
  479:       els: [].slice.call(stage.querySelectorAll(':scope > .el')).map(function (el) {
  480:         var body = el.querySelector('.txt .body');
  481:         return {
  482:           state: read(el),
  483:           html: body ? body.innerHTML : null, // the runs live in the DOM, so they are part of the state
  484:           sel: el === selected,
  485:         };
  486:       }),
  487:     };
  488:   }
  489: 
  490:   /* Returns TRUE when the stage now holds `snap`, and FALSE when it refused it -- and on false the
  491:    * stage is byte-for-byte as it was. The caller owns the history stacks and may only move `snap`
  492:    * off one of them once this has returned true; see undo() and redo(). */
  493:   function restore(snap) {
  494:     /* PASS 1 -- JUDGE. Nothing above this line has touched the DOM, and nothing below it may run if
  495:      * the snapshot is refused. stopEditing() is DOM mutation (it drops .editing and removes the
  496:      * contenteditable attribute) and it calls endTx(), so it belongs BELOW the preflight, not
  497:      * above: "before touching the DOM" means before stopEditing(), not merely before the clear. */
  498:     var why = unrestorableReason(snap);
  499:     if (why) {
  500:       setStatus('That history step was refused, so nothing changed: ' + why);
  501:       return false;
  502:     }
  503: 
  504:     /* PASS 2 -- COMMIT. Every record is legal, so this cannot fail half-way through. */
  505:     stopEditing();
  506:     [].slice.call(stage.querySelectorAll(':scope > .el')).forEach(function (n) { n.remove(); });
  507:     // The node `selected` pointed at has just been removed, so the identity check at the top of
  508:     // select() would short-circuit against a corpse. Clear it before anything can consult it.
  509:     selected = null;
  510:     titleField.innerHTML = snap.title;
  511: 
  512:     var toSelect = null;
  513:     for (var i = 0; i < snap.els.length; i++) {
  514:       var rec = snap.els[i];
  515:       var el = makeEl(rec.state); // NOT commit(): a restore must not be TRANSFORMED -- not
  516:       stage.appendChild(el);      // re-snapped, not clamped, not pushed back onto the stack it came
  517:                                   // from. It HAS been judged, by unrestorableReason(), above.
  518:       var body = el.querySelector('.txt .body');
  519:       if (body && rec.html !== null) body.innerHTML = rec.html;
  520:       if (rec.sel) toSelect = el;
  521:     }
  522:     select(toSelect);
  523:     syncProps(); // select(null) short-circuits when nothing was selected, so ask for this outright
  524:     return true;
  525:   }
  526: 
  527:   function pushUndo(before) {
  528:     past.push(before);
  529:     if (past.length > HIST_MAX) past.shift();
  530:     future.length = 0; // a fresh edit forks the timeline -- the redos are unreachable now
  531:     syncHistory();
  532:   }
  533: 
  534:   function beginTx() {
  535:     if (txOpen) return; // a gesture already owns the step; do not start a second one inside it
  536:     txOpen = true;
  537:     txBefore = snapshot();
  538:     txPushed = false;
  539:   }
  540: 
  541:   /* Call AFTER a mutation has succeeded, never before. `before` is the pre-mutation snapshot, and
  542:    * it is only needed for changes that happen OUTSIDE a gesture -- inside one, txBefore is it. */
  543:   function noteChange(before) {
  544:     if (txOpen) {
  545:       if (!txPushed) { pushUndo(txBefore); txPushed = true; }
  546:       return;
  547:     }
  548:     if (before) pushUndo(before);
  549:   }
  550: 
  551:   function endTx() { txOpen = false; txBefore = null; txPushed = false; }
  552: 
  553:   /* PEEK, THEN POP -- never `restore(past.pop())`.
  554:    *
  555:    * A pop written as the argument is evaluated BEFORE the callee runs, so the entry is off the stack
  556:    * before restore() has done anything with it. If the restore is then refused, or throws on a
  557:    * malformed record, the stage has been cleared AND the only copy of the state that would put it
  558:    * back has been destroyed: the user loses the deck and the undo step in one keystroke. So the
  559:    * entry comes off the stack once the stage actually holds it, and not one statement sooner.
  560:    *
  561:    * A refused entry therefore STAYS on the stack. That is the fail-closed half of the trade: Ctrl+Z
  562:    * declining to move is a bug report, whereas silently discarding the user's only copy of a state
  563:    * is a bug we never hear about because the evidence went with it.
  564:    */
  565:   function undo() {
  566:     if (!past.length) { setStatus('Nothing to undo.'); return; }
  567:     endTx(); // a gesture may not straddle an undo
  568:     var now = snapshot();
  569:     if (!restore(past[past.length - 1])) return; // refused: restore() said why, and nothing moved
  570:     past.pop();
  571:     future.push(now);
  572:     syncHistory();
  573:     setStatus('Undo ??' + past.length + ' step(s) left.');
  574:   }
  575: 
  576:   function redo() {
  577:     if (!future.length) { setStatus('Nothing to redo.'); return; }
  578:     endTx();
  579:     var now = snapshot();
  580:     if (!restore(future[future.length - 1])) return;
  581:     future.pop();
  582:     past.push(now);
  583:     syncHistory();
  584:     setStatus('Redo ??' + future.length + ' step(s) left.');
  585:   }
  586: 
  587:   function syncHistory() {
  588:     $('b-undo').disabled = !past.length;
  589:     $('b-redo').disabled = !future.length;
  590:   }
  591:

  594:   function esc(v) { return String(v).replace(/&/g, '&amp;').replace(/</g, '&lt;').replace(/"/g, '&quot;'); }
  595: 
  596:   // No fill => fill="none". No stroke => the stroke attribute is OMITTED ENTIRELY. Never

  610:       case 'triangle':

  705:   function handleHTML(h) { return '<div class="handle" data-h="' + h + '"></div>'; }
  706: 
  707:   function makeEl(s) {
  708:     var el = document.createElement('div');
  709:     el.className = 'el';
  710:     var html = '<svg xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" preserveAspectRatio="none"></svg>';
  711: 
  712:     if (s.type === 'connector') {
  713:       html += handleHTML('p1') + handleHTML('p2');
  714:     } else {
  715:       // .txt is the flex/anchor box (the Sec 5.6 contract); .body is the text. One flex item, so
  716:       // multiple paragraphs stack instead of laying out side by side.
  717:       //
  718:       // NOTE THE ABSENT contenteditable. It is added by the dblclick handler and removed by
  719:       // stopEditing(). It used to be hard-coded here, with only `pointer-events: none` on .txt
  720:       // keeping the caret out -- which meant the element was, to the keyboard and to every
  721:       // assistive technology, permanently editable, and editingText() could not tell "the caret is
  722:       // in the text" from "the text merely exists".
  723:       html += '<div class="txt"><div class="body"></div></div>';
  724:       for (var i = 0; i < HANDLES.length; i++) html += handleHTML(HANDLES[i]);
  725:       html += handleHTML('rot');
  726:     }
  727:     el.innerHTML = html;
  728:     write(el, s);
  729:     renderEl(el);
  730:     wire(el);
  731:     return el;
  732:   }
  733: 
  734:   // Z-ORDER IS DOM ORDER AND NOTHING ELSE. No `z` field, no CSS stacking index on .el, no sort key
  735:   // -- any of those would be a second source of truth and would desync. Raise/lower are pure
  736:   // insertBefore/append.
  737:   // New connectors go to the FRONT of the array -- drawn first, therefore BEHIND the boxes they
  738:   // join. Front of array == back of the slide.
  739:   function addEl(el) {
  740:     if (el.dataset.type === 'connector') {
  741:       var firstShape = stage.querySelector(':scope > .el:not([data-type="connector"])');
  742:       stage.insertBefore(el, firstShape); // null => append
  743:     } else {
  744:       stage.appendChild(el);
  745:     }
  746:     return el;
  747:   }

  749:   /* [REDACTED] interactions */
  750: 
  751:   function wire(el) {
  752:     // Drag the body. The text and the handles are excluded: a pointerdown in the text must land a
  753:     // caret, and one on a handle must resize.
  754:     // select() FIRST, then beginTx(): select() closes any open text session, and closing one ends
  755:     // its transaction. Opening ours before that would hand our snapshot straight to endTx().
  756:     Interactions.draggable(el, {
  757:       ignoreFrom: '.txt, .handle',
  758:       enabled: function () { return tool === 'select'; },
  759:       start: function () { select(el); beginTx(); },
  760:       end: endTx,
  761:       move: function (ctx) {
  762:         var s = read(el);
  763:         // Device px -> logical units. ALWAYS. This is the corollary of the Prime Directive.
  764:         var dx = ctx.dx / zoom, dy = ctx.dy / zoom;
  765:         if (s.type === 'connector') {
  766:           commit(el, { x1: s.x1 + dx, y1: s.y1 + dy, x2: s.x2 + dx, y2: s.y2 + dy },
  767:                  { alt: ctx.pointer.altKey });
  768:         } else {
  769:           commit(el, { x: s.x + dx, y: s.y + dy }, { alt: ctx.pointer.altKey });
  770:         }
  771:       },
  772:     });
  773: 
  774:     if (el.dataset.type === 'connector') {
  775:       ['p1', 'p2'].forEach(function (which) {
  776:         var h = el.querySelector('.handle[data-h="' + which + '"]');
  777:         Interactions.pointerDrag(h, {
  778:           start: function () { select(el); beginTx(); },
  779:           end: endTx,
  780:           move: function (ctx) {
  781:             // Endpoints drag individually. NO GLUING -- begin_connect()/end_connect() are out of
  782:             // Phase 1 (D-3).
  783:             var p = toModel(ctx.pointer);
  784:             var patch = which === 'p1' ? { x1: p.x, y1: p.y } : { x2: p.x, y2: p.y };
  785:             commit(el, patch, { alt: ctx.pointer.altKey });
  786:           },
  787:         });
  788:       });
  789:       return;
  790:     }
  791: 
  792:     HANDLES.forEach(function (h) {
  793:       var node = el.querySelector('.handle[data-h="' + h + '"]');
  794:       Interactions.resizable(node, h, {
  795:         start: function () { select(el); beginTx(); },
  796:         end: endTx,
  797:         move: function (ctx) {
  798:           var s = read(el);
  799:           // deltaRect is DEVICE PIXELS, exactly as interact.js reports it. Each field is divided
  800:           // by zoom, in the same expression, or the whole thing breaks the moment zoom != 1.
  801:           var nx = s.x + ctx.deltaRect.left / zoom;
  802:           var ny = s.y + ctx.deltaRect.top / zoom;
  803:           var nw = s.w + ctx.deltaRect.width / zoom;
  804:           var nh = s.h + ctx.deltaRect.height / zoom;
  805:           commit(el, { x: nx, y: ny, w: nw, h: nh },
  806:                  { alt: ctx.pointer.altKey, edges: ctx.edges });
  807:         },
  808:       });
  809:     });
  810: 
  811:     var rotHandle = el.querySelector('.handle[data-h="rot"]');
  812:     Interactions.pointerDrag(rotHandle, {
  813:       start: function () { select(el); beginTx(); },
  814:       end: endTx,
  815:       move: function (ctx) {
  816:         var s = read(el);
  817:         var p = toModel(ctx.pointer);
  818:         var cx = s.x + s.w / 2, cy = s.y + s.h / 2;
  819:         // +90 so that "handle straight up" reads as 0 degrees.
  820:         var deg = (Math.atan2(p.y - cy, p.x - cx) * 180) / Math.PI + 90;
  821:         if (ctx.pointer.shiftKey) deg = Math.round(deg / 15) * 15;
  822:         deg = ((deg + 180) % 360 + 360) % 360 - 180; // normalize to (-180, 180]
  823:         commit(el, { rot: round6(deg) }, { alt: ctx.pointer.altKey });
  824:         syncProps();
  825:       },
  826:     });
  827: 
  828:     el.addEventListener('pointerdown', function () { if (tool === 'select') select(el); });
  829: 
  830:     // Typing is a mutation like any other, and it is grouped like any other: the session opened by
  831:     // the dblclick below is ONE undo step, however many keystrokes it holds.
  832:     var textBody = el.querySelector('.txt .body');
  833:     if (textBody) textBody.addEventListener('input', function () { noteChange(); });
  834: 
  835:     // Click selects and drags; DOUBLE-click opens the text. Without this a no-fill textbox could
  836:     // never be dragged at all -- its .txt covers the whole element and would eat every grab.
  837:     el.addEventListener('dblclick', function (ev) {
  838:       if (tool !== 'select' || el.dataset.type === 'connector') return;
  839:       ev.preventDefault();
  840:       select(el);
  841:       var body = el.querySelector('.txt .body');
  842:       if (!body) return;
  843:       // THE ATTRIBUTE, not merely the class. `.el.editing .txt { pointer-events: auto }` decides
  844:       // whether a POINTER can reach the text; contenteditable decides whether the element is
  845:       // editable at all. Only the second one is what editingText() -- and the keyboard, and a screen
  846:       // reader -- actually consults.
  847:       body.setAttribute('contenteditable', 'true');
  848:       el.classList.add('editing');
  849:       beginTx(); // the whole editing session is one step; stopEditing() closes it
  850:       body.focus();
  851:     });
  852:   }
  853: 
  854:   function stopEditing() {
  855:     var open = stage.querySelectorAll('.el.editing');
  856:     for (var i = 0; i < open.length; i++) {
  857:       open[i].classList.remove('editing');
  858:       // Drop the attribute with the class. An element that is not being edited is not editable --
  859:       // leaving it behind is what made `contenteditable` permanent in the first place.
  860:       var body = open[i].querySelector('.txt .body');
  861:       if (body) body.removeAttribute('contenteditable');
  862:     }
  863:     // The session ends here. If nothing was typed, txPushed is false and the snapshot is simply
  864:     // dropped -- opening the text and closing it again is not an edit.
  865:     endTx();
  866:   }
  867: 
  868:   /* Creation by drag-out on the stage. Pointer coords via toModel(), then the SAME pipeline -- so
  869:      an illegal shape cannot be drawn in the first place. */
  870:   Interactions.pointerDrag(stage, {
  871:     filter: function (ev) {
  872:       if (tool === 'select') return false;
  873:       // A drawing tool draws, even over an existing element -- the alternative is that the user
  874:       // cannot put a label on top of a box. Only the title field keeps its caret.
  875:       return !ev.target.closest('#title-field');
  876:     },
  877:     start: function (ctx) {
  878:       ctx.origin = toModel(ctx.pointer);
  879:       ctx.made = null;
  880:       // NOT beginTx(). The moment this gesture creates its element it calls select(), and select()
  881:       // closes any open text session -- which would end our transaction and take the snapshot with
  882:       // it. So the draw path carries its own `before` and pushes it exactly once, at creation.
  883:       ctx.before = snapshot();
  884:     },
  885:     move: function (ctx) {
  886:       var p = toModel(ctx.pointer);
  887:       var o = ctx.origin;
  888:       var cand;
  889: 
  890:       if (tool === 'connector') {
  891:         var d = DEFAULTS.connector;
  892:         cand = {
  893:           id: ctx.made ? ctx.made.dataset.id : nextId(), type: 'connector',
  894:           x1: o.x, y1: o.y, x2: p.x, y2: p.y,
  895:           kind: d.kind, arrow: d.arrow, color: d.color, width: d.width,
  896:         };
  897:       } else {
  898:         var def = tool === 'textbox' ? DEFAULTS.textbox : DEFAULTS.shape;
  899:         cand = {
  900:           id: ctx.made ? ctx.made.dataset.id : nextId(), type: tool,
  901:           x: Math.min(o.x, p.x), y: Math.min(o.y, p.y),
  902:           w: Math.abs(p.x - o.x), h: Math.abs(p.y - o.y), rot: 0,
  903:           fill: def.fill, strokeColor: def.strokeColor, strokeWidth: def.strokeWidth,
  904:           size: def.size, color: def.color, align: def.align, valign: def.valign,
  905:           bold: def.bold, italic: def.italic,
  906:         };
  907:       }
  908: 
  909:       var edges = { left: true, right: true, top: true, bottom: true };
  910:       var legal = applyConstraints(cand, { alt: ctx.pointer.altKey, edges: edges });
  911:       if (!legal) {
  912:         if (ctx.made) flashBlocked(ctx.made);
  913:         return; // an illegal shape simply does not get drawn
  914:       }
  915: 
  916:       if (!ctx.made) {
  917:         ctx.made = addEl(makeEl(legal));
  918:         select(ctx.made);
  919:         // ONE step for the whole drag-out: the element and the size it was drawn at are a single
  920:         // act, and undoing it should leave no husk behind. Every later frame of this gesture goes
  921:         // down the write()/renderEl() path below, which pushes nothing.
  922:         pushUndo(ctx.before);
  923:       } else {
  924:         write(ctx.made, legal);
  925:         renderEl(ctx.made);
  926:       }
  927:     },
  928:     end: function (ctx) {
  929:       if (!ctx.made) {
  930:         setStatus('Nothing drawn ??drag out a shape rather than clicking.');
  931:         return;
  932:       }
  933:       setTool('select');
  934:       syncProps();
  935:       setStatus('Created ' + ctx.made.dataset.id + '.');
  936:     },
  937:   });
  938: 
  939:   stage.addEventListener('pointerdown', function (ev) {
  940:     if (tool === 'select' && !ev.target.closest('.el') && !ev.target.closest('#title-field')) {

 succeeded in 1234ms:
    1: /* editor.js ??the DOM -> canvas-ir/1 visual editor. Plan Sec 5, tasks 2.1 - 2.8.
    2:  *
    3:  * ============================================================================================
    4:  * THE PRIME DIRECTIVE
    5:  *
    6:  *   The stage's device rect is read EXACTLY ONCE, in toModel(), and only ever to invert a
    7:  *   pointer position. Nothing else in this file measures the DOM. Ever.
    8:  *
    9:  * Geometry lives in JS state (el.dataset) and flows ONE WAY:
   10:  *
   11:  *     state (dataset x/y/w/h/rot)  ??render??>  CSS + SVG attributes
   12:  *                                  ??never read back ?? *
   13:  * Every measured-geometry API in the browser is BANNED here, as is any percentage width in the IR
   14:  * path and any value read back out of CSS. The enforced list lives in
   15:  * tests/canvas/test_editor_source.py (Gate 1) -- deliberately, so that the test is the spec and
   16:  * this comment cannot drift away from it. Gate 1 is a plain substring grep over this file, which
   17:  * is why the banned names are not spelled out here: naming them would trip the very test that
   18:  * enforces them. That test is the only thing standing between "we agreed on the rule" and "the
   19:  * rule held".
   20:  *
   21:  * The EMU-per-unit constant does not appear here either. The browser never converts to EMU; Python
   22:  * does, once, at the end. We carry floats and round only to kill float noise (round6).
   23:  * ============================================================================================
   24:  */
   25: 
   26: (function () {
   27:   'use strict';
   28: 
   29:   /* [REDACTED] authoritative constants */
   30: 
   31:   // Echoed into every payload so the backend can reject a STALE deck instead of silently rescaling
   32:   // it. There is exactly one model space and the renderer NEVER rescales.
   33:   var CANVAS = Object.freeze({ w: 960, h: 540, unit: 'pt' });
   34:   var SCHEMA = 'canvas-ir/1';
   35: 
   36:   // Ported VERBATIM from canvas/geometry.py. Same numbers, same half-open rule, same rotated AABB.
   37:   // If these two ever disagree, the user is looking at a legal-looking stage and a 422 they cannot
   38:   // explain.
   39:   var HEADER_RESERVED = [0, 0, 960, 92];
   40:   var FOOTER_RESERVED = [733.6, 500, 960, 540];
   41:   var BODY_SAFE = [45.6, 92, 914.4, 500]; // SNAP TARGET ONLY -- never a constraint
   42:   var REJECT_REGIONS = [
   43:     { name: 'HEADER_RESERVED', box: HEADER_RESERVED },
   44:     { name: 'FOOTER_RESERVED', box: FOOTER_RESERVED },
   45:   ];
   46:   var EPS = 1e-9; // == geometry.EPS: a rotated corner can land on 960.0000000000001
   47: 
   48:   var BRAND_RED = '#FF0032';
   49:   // Theme accent1. validate.py REJECTS it, so the colour picker rejects it too -- the user finds
   50:   // out here, in the editor, and not in a 422 they cannot explain. It is referenced ONLY by the
   51:   // colour guard (guardColor, below): never as a default, a palette entry or a fallback.
   52:   var FORBIDDEN_COLOR = '#4472C4';
   53: 
   54:   var MIN_EXTENT = 4; // schema requires > 0; the editor requires >= 4
   55:   var GRID = 4;
   56:   var SNAP_TOL = 3; // model units
   57:   var ZOOM_MIN = 25;
   58:   var ZOOM_MAX = 200;
   59: 
   60:   // Snap guides, in MODEL space: the grid, the BODY_SAFE edges, the stage edges, the centre lines.
   61:   var GUIDES_X = [0, BODY_SAFE[0], CANVAS.w / 2, BODY_SAFE[2], CANVAS.w];
   62:   var GUIDES_Y = [0, BODY_SAFE[1], CANVAS.h / 2, BODY_SAFE[3], CANVAS.h];
   63: 
   64:   // bold/italic are ELEMENT-level defaults for the runs inside, exactly as size and color already
   65:   // are. The IR has no element-level bold -- it has run.bold -- so these are folded into every run
   66:   // by normalizeText() on the way out and summarised back off the runs by stateFromIR() on the way
   67:   // in. See setTextFlag() for why "off" also has to strip <b>/<i> out of the body.
   68:   var DEFAULTS = {
   69:     shape: { fill: BRAND_RED, strokeColor: '', strokeWidth: 1, size: 14, color: '#FFFFFF', align: 'CENTER', valign: 'MIDDLE', bold: false, italic: false },
   70:     // A textbox is geometrically a rect; the split is authorial intent. No fill, black ink.
   71:     textbox: { fill: '', strokeColor: '', strokeWidth: 1, size: 14, color: '#000000', align: 'CENTER', valign: 'MIDDLE', bold: false, italic: false },
   72:     connector: { color: BRAND_RED, width: 1.5, arrow: 'end', kind: 'straight' },
   73:   };
   74: 
   75:   var SHAPE_TYPES = ['rect', 'ellipse', 'triangle', 'textbox'];
   76:   var HANDLES = ['nw', 'n', 'ne', 'e', 'se', 's', 'sw', 'w'];
   77: 
   78:   /* [REDACTED] the DOM */
   79: 
   80:   var stage = document.getElementById('stage');
   81:   var stageWrap = document.getElementById('stage-wrap');
   82:   var titleField = document.getElementById('title-field');
   83:   var warnings = document.getElementById('warnings');
   84:   var statusMsg = document.getElementById('status-msg');
   85:   var props = document.getElementById('props'); // the right-hand panel; hidden with nothing selected
   86: 
   87:   var zoom = 1; // THE single source of truth for scale. --zoom is written FROM it, never read.
   88:   var tool = 'select';
   89:   var selected = null;
   90:   var CONTEXT = null; // from /api/context. Never a hard-coded SHA.
   91:   var idSeq = 0;
   92: 
   93:   /* =========================================================================================
   94:    * THE ONLY PLACE A DEVICE COORDINATE BECOMES A MODEL COORDINATE.
   95:    * ========================================================================================= */
   96:   function toModel(ev) {
   97:     var r = stage.getBoundingClientRect(); // used ONLY for pointer inversion
   98:     return { x: (ev.clientX - r.left) / zoom, y: (ev.clientY - r.top) / zoom };
   99:   }
  100: 
  101:   /* [REDACTED] geometry: a port of geometry.py */
  102: 
  103:   // Strict overlap. HALF-OPEN: edge contact is NOT intersection. An element whose top edge is
  104:   // exactly y == 92 is LEGAL -- BODY_SAFE starts at exactly 92. Get this wrong and every
  105:   // top-aligned element the user draws is spuriously blocked. Same at y == 500.
  106:   function intersects(a, b) {
  107:     return a[0] < b[2] && a[2] > b[0] && a[1] < b[3] && a[3] > b[1];
  108:   }
  109: 
  110:   // The ROTATED axis-aligned bounding box -- NOT the path box. A 45-degree box whose unrotated
  111:   // footprint clears the header but whose rotated corners do not is rejected by the backend, so it
  112:   // is rejected here too. y grows DOWNWARD, so this matrix turns CLOCKWISE on screen, which is what
  113:   // DrawingML's a:xfrm/@rot means.
  114:   function elementBBox(s) {
  115:     if (s.type === 'connector') {
  116:       return [
  117:         Math.min(s.x1, s.x2), Math.min(s.y1, s.y2),
  118:         Math.max(s.x1, s.x2), Math.max(s.y1, s.y2),
  119:       ];
  120:     }
  121:     var x = s.x, y = s.y, w = s.w, h = s.h;
  122:     var rot = s.rot || 0;
  123:     if (rot % 360 === 0) return [x, y, x + w, y + h];
  124: 
  125:     var cx = x + w / 2, cy = y + h / 2;
  126:     var t = (rot * Math.PI) / 180;
  127:     var cos = Math.cos(t), sin = Math.sin(t);
  128:     var corners = [[x, y], [x + w, y], [x + w, y + h], [x, y + h]];
  129:     var xs = [], ys = [];
  130:     for (var i = 0; i < corners.length; i++) {
  131:       var dx = corners[i][0] - cx, dy = corners[i][1] - cy;
  132:       xs.push(cx + dx * cos - dy * sin);
  133:       ys.push(cy + dx * sin + dy * cos);
  134:     }
  135:     return [Math.min.apply(null, xs), Math.min.apply(null, ys),
  136:             Math.max.apply(null, xs), Math.max.apply(null, ys)];
  137:   }
  138: 
  139:   function outOfBounds(s) {
  140:     var b = elementBBox(s);
  141:     return b[0] < -EPS || b[1] < -EPS || b[2] > CANVAS.w + EPS || b[3] > CANVAS.h + EPS;
  142:   }
  143: 
  144:   function clamp(v, lo, hi) { return Math.min(hi, Math.max(lo, v)); }
  145:   function round6(v) { return Math.round(v * 1e6) / 1e6; }
  146: 
  147:   /* [REDACTED] snapping (MODEL space) */
  148: 
  149:   // interact.js's snap/restrict modifiers are NOT used: they operate in device pixels, so at
  150:   // zoom = 0.5 a 4-unit grid quietly becomes a 2-unit grid and a restriction lands in the wrong
  151:   // place. Everything here runs AFTER the / zoom conversion, on model numbers.
  152: 
  153:   function snapVal(v, guides) {
  154:     for (var i = 0; i < guides.length; i++) {
  155:       if (Math.abs(v - guides[i]) <= SNAP_TOL) return guides[i];
  156:     }
  157:     return Math.round(v / GRID) * GRID;
  158:   }
  159: 
  160:   // Snap a MOVE: the size is fixed, so the leading, centre and trailing edges are all candidates
  161:   // and the closest wins.
  162:   function snapAxis(v, size, guides) {
  163:     var best = null, bestD = SNAP_TOL;
  164:     var anchors = [[v, 0], [v + size / 2, size / 2], [v + size, size]];
  165:     for (var g = 0; g < guides.length; g++) {
  166:       for (var a = 0; a < anchors.length; a++) {
  167:         var d = Math.abs(anchors[a][0] - guides[g]);
  168:         if (d < bestD) { bestD = d; best = guides[g] - anchors[a][1]; }
  169:       }
  170:     }
  171:     return best !== null ? best : Math.round(v / GRID) * GRID;
  172:   }
  173: 
  174:   /* [REDACTED] the constraint pipeline */
  175: 
  176:   /* Every committed frame, in this order:
  177:    *   1. candidate state from the delta (the caller did that)
  178:    *   2. w, h >= 4
  179:    *   3. snap (unless Alt)
  180:    *   4. clamp to the slide
  181:    *   5. rotated AABB vs HEADER_RESERVED / FOOTER_RESERVED
  182:    *   6. legal -> return it. Illegal -> return null, and the caller HOLDS ITS LAST LEGAL STATE.
  183:    *
  184:    * REJECT-AND-HOLD, not push-out. A "clever" push-out under rotation is non-deterministic and it
  185:    * will disagree with geometry.py -- at which point the user is looking at a legal-looking stage
  186:    * and a 422.
  187:    *
  188:    * STEPS 2-4 TRANSFORM A CANDIDATE. STEPS 5-6 JUDGE IT. They are two different jobs, so they are
  189:    * two different functions -- because the JSON import path must reuse the JUDGE and must never
  190:    * reuse the TRANSFORM. Snapping, growing and clamping a file the user hand-authored would
  191:    * silently rewrite their deck on load, which is the same push-out we refused to do for the mouse.
  192:    * illegalReason() below is the judge, and it is the ONLY door into the stage: the mouse goes
  193:    * through it via applyConstraints(), a file goes through it via loadIR(), and the undo stack goes
  194:    * through it via unrestorableReason(). Three writers, one judge. A fourth writer that skips it
  195:    * would not be a new feature with a bug in it -- it would be a hole in requirement 4, and the
  196:    * reserved-region guarantee (the thing the backend returns 422 on) would die inside it, quietly.
  197:    */
  198: 
  199:   // The geometry a type carries, and the ONLY fields the judge reads.
  200:   var GEOM_FIELDS = {
  201:     connector: ['x1', 'y1', 'x2', 'y2'],
  202:     shape: ['x', 'y', 'w', 'h', 'rot'],
  203:   };
  204: 
  205:   var ELEMENT_TYPES = SHAPE_TYPES.concat(['connector']);
  206: 
  207:   /* THE NUMERIC GUARD. This is not paranoia -- it is the hole that every geometry check has.
  208:    *
  209:    * EVERY comparison against NaN is false. So if x is NaN then outOfBounds() returns FALSE and
  210:    * intersects() returns FALSE, and a garbage element sails straight through a rule that is
  211:    * otherwise perfectly correct. A missing coordinate becomes +undefined === NaN the moment read()
  212:    * touches it, and a file is allowed to be missing a coordinate. The judge therefore proves the
  213:    * numbers are numbers BEFORE it compares them.
  214:    *
  215:    * typeof also disposes of true and of "100": the payload schema says `number`, so a boolean or a
  216:    * string coordinate is a 422 in waiting, not a coordinate. validate.py::_num draws this exact
  217:    * line on the other side, and for this exact reason.
  218:    */
  219:   function geometryIsReadable(s) {
  220:     var fields = s.type === 'connector' ? GEOM_FIELDS.connector : GEOM_FIELDS.shape;
  221:     for (var i = 0; i < fields.length; i++) {
  222:       var v = s[fields[i]];
  223:       if (typeof v !== 'number' || !isFinite(v)) return false;
  224:     }
  225:     return true;
  226:   }
  227: 
  228:   /* THE LEGALITY RULE -- the one judge, and the whole of requirement 4.
  229:    *
  230:    * Returns a human reason this state may not exist on the stage, or null when it may. It reads
  231:    * state and it TRANSFORMS NOTHING: a judge that quietly repairs its defendant is not a judge, and
  232:    * the import path can only borrow this half precisely because it is inert.
  233:    *
  234:    * MIN_EXTENT is deliberately NOT enforced here. w >= 4 is drawing ergonomics -- you cannot mouse
  235:    * out a 2-unit box -- whereas the schema's rule, and therefore the legality rule, is w > 0. If
  236:    * this function enforced 4 then loading a legal 2-unit element would have to either reject a deck
  237:    * the backend accepts or resize the user's file. It enforces > 0, which is the rule that is
  238:    * actually true on both sides.
  239:    */
  240:   function illegalReason(s) {
  241:     if (ELEMENT_TYPES.indexOf(s.type) < 0) {
  242:       return 'has an unknown type -- expected one of ' + ELEMENT_TYPES.join(', ');
  243:     }
  244:     if (!geometryIsReadable(s)) {
  245:       return 'has a missing or non-numeric coordinate, so its box cannot be computed at all';
  246:     }
  247:     if (s.type !== 'connector' && (s.w <= 0 || s.h <= 0)) {
  248:       // An inverted box is not a small box. elementBBox() would hand back x2 < x1, and every
  249:       // predicate downstream would then be comparing an interval that runs backwards -- which is a
  250:       // second way to be waved through a correct check. Refuse it before it can lie.
  251:       return 'has a non-positive width or height';
  252:     }
  253:     if (outOfBounds(s)) {
  254:       return 'falls outside the ' + CANVAS.w + 'x' + CANVAS.h + ' slide';
  255:     }
  256:     var box = elementBBox(s);
  257:     for (var i = 0; i < REJECT_REGIONS.length; i++) {
  258:       if (intersects(box, REJECT_REGIONS[i].box)) {
  259:         return 'intersects ' + REJECT_REGIONS[i].name + ' -- that region carries inherited layout ' +
  260:                'chrome (the logo, the divider, the page number) which always renders BEHIND slide ' +
  261:                'content and cannot be raised above it. Move it into the body, y 92 -> 500.';
  262:       }
  263:     }
  264:     return null;
  265:   }
  266: 
  267:   /* THE THIRD CALLER OF THE JUDGE -- restore(), i.e. the undo stack.
  268:    *
  269:    * A restore must not be TRANSFORMED: not snapped, not clamped, not re-pushed onto the stack it
  270:    * came from. That is true, and it is why restore() calls makeEl() and not commit(). It does not
  271:    * follow that a restore must not be JUDGED. illegalReason() transforms nothing -- that inertness
  272:    * is the entire reason it exists as its own function, apart from applyConstraints() -- so judging
  273:    * a snapshot costs it nothing and takes nothing away from it. Judging is free.
  274:    *
  275:    * ALL OR NOTHING, and in this order: judge every record FIRST, touch the stage SECOND. The same
  276:    * order, for the same reason, as loadIR(). restore() clears the stage before it has looked at a
  277:    * single element, so a per-element check bolted into its loop -- however correct that check was --
  278:    * could only ever leave a half-restored stage behind and the user's own deck already gone.
  279:    * "Refuse the entire restore" is not reachable from inside that loop. It is reachable from here.
  280:    *
  281:    * WHY THIS RUNS AT ALL, given that every element in the DOM was judged on the way in: because that
  282:    * is an INDUCTIVE argument, and it is only as strong as its weakest step. It holds today because
  283:    * commit(), loadIR() and duplicateSelected() are the only three writers of dataset geometry and
  284:    * all three call the judge. The next writer -- a paste handler, an alignment tool, an arrow-key
  285:    * nudge -- inherits an undo stack that will happily reintroduce whatever it wrote, in a function
  286:    * nobody suspects. The claim on line 193 is unconditional. This is what makes it true.
  287:    *
  288:    * It READS AND RETURNS: it mutates nothing and it touches no DOM. (The Gate 4b harness runs it
  289:    * with no `stage` and no `titleField` defined at all, so any DOM access here is a ReferenceError
  290:    * under 'use strict' -- which is how "it touches no DOM" is an assertion and not a hope.)
  291:    *
  292:    * Returns a human reason this snapshot may not be put on the stage, or null when it may.
  293:    */
  294:   function unrestorableReason(snap) {
  295:     if (!snap || typeof snap !== 'object' || !Array.isArray(snap.els) || typeof snap.title !== 'string') {
  296:       return 'that history entry is not a snapshot of a stage';
  297:     }
  298: 
  299:     var problems = [];

  301:       var rec = snap.els[i];

  303:         problems.push('els[' + i + ']: is not an element record');

  305:       }

  310:       }
  311:     }
  312:     if (!problems.length) return null;
  313: 
  314:     return problems.length + ' element(s) in that history step may not be placed on this stage:\n' +
  315:            problems.map(function (p, n) { return '  ' + (n + 1) + '. ' + p; }).join('\n');
  316:   }

  318:   function applyConstraints(s, opt) {
  319:     opt = opt || {};
  320:     var c = {};
  321:     for (var k in s) c[k] = s[k];
  322: 
  323:     if (c.type === 'connector') {

  326:         c.x2 = snapVal(c.x2, GUIDES_X); c.y2 = snapVal(c.y2, GUIDES_Y);
  327:       }
  328:       c.x1 = clamp(c.x1, 0, CANVAS.w); c.x2 = clamp(c.x2, 0, CANVAS.w);
  329:       c.y1 = clamp(c.y1, 0, CANVAS.h); c.y2 = clamp(c.y2, 0, CANVAS.h);
  330:     } else {
  331:       // 2. minimum extent
  332:       c.w = Math.max(MIN_EXTENT, c.w);
  333:       c.h = Math.max(MIN_EXTENT, c.h);
  334: 
  335:       // 3. snap, in model space
  336:       if (!opt.alt) {
  337:         var px = c.x, pw = c.w, py = c.y, ph = c.h;
  338:         if (opt.edges) {
  339:           // A resize or a drag-out: snap only the edges that actually moved. Snapping x during an
  340:           // SE-handle resize would slide the element instead of resizing it.
  341:           var x1 = c.x, x2 = c.x + c.w, y1 = c.y, y2 = c.y + c.h;
  342:           if (opt.edges.left) x1 = snapVal(x1, GUIDES_X);
  343:           if (opt.edges.right) x2 = snapVal(x2, GUIDES_X);
  344:           if (opt.edges.top) y1 = snapVal(y1, GUIDES_Y);
  345:           if (opt.edges.bottom) y2 = snapVal(y2, GUIDES_Y);
  346:           c.x = x1; c.w = x2 - x1; c.y = y1; c.h = y2 - y1;

  348:           c.x = snapAxis(c.x, c.w, GUIDES_X);
  349:           c.y = snapAxis(c.y, c.h, GUIDES_Y);
  350:         }
  351:         // Snapping must never talk the box below the minimum. If it did, undo it on that axis --
  352:         // the invariant outranks the convenience.
  353:         if (c.w < MIN_EXTENT) { c.x = px; c.w = pw; }
  354:         if (c.h < MIN_EXTENT) { c.y = py; c.h = ph; }

  356: 
  357:       // 4. clamp the ROTATED footprint into the slide, by translating.

  359:       if (b[2] - b[0] > CANVAS.w + EPS || b[3] - b[1] > CANVAS.h + EPS) return null;
  360:       var sx = 0, sy = 0;

 1612:   /* An IR element -> editor state.
 1613:    *
 1614:    * It NEVER throws and it NEVER repairs, and both halves of that are load-bearing.
 1615:    *
 1616:    * Not throwing is what lets the gate judge the WHOLE file and report every bad element at once,

 1625:   function stateFromIR(e) {

 1627: 
 1628:     if (e.type === 'connector') {
 1629:       var cd = DEFAULTS.connector;
 1630:       return {

 1635:         color: e.color || cd.color,
 1636:         width: typeof e.width === 'number' ? e.width : cd.width,
 1637:       };
 1638:     }
 1639: 
 1640:     var text = e.text && typeof e.text === 'object' ? e.text : {};
 1641:     var paras = Array.isArray(text.paragraphs) ? text.paragraphs : [];

 1645:     var stroke = e.stroke && typeof e.stroke === 'object' ? e.stroke : null;
 1646: 
 1647:     /* The element's bold/italic flags are a SUMMARY of the runs: on only when EVERY run is bold.
 1648:      *
 1649:      * Reading them off the FIRST run -- the way size and color are read -- would be wrong here, and
 1650:      * destructively so. The flag is OR-ed into every run on the way back out (normalizeText), so
 1651:      * "the first run is bold" would mean a deck with one bold word in a plain sentence reloads with
 1652:      * the flag on, and re-exports with the WHOLE sentence bold. A round trip must not change the
 1653:      * deck. Mixed runs therefore summarise to OFF, and their own <b>/<i> tags -- which
 1654:      * paragraphsToHTML() writes -- carry the per-run bold instead. Nothing is lost either way.
 1655:      *
 1656:      * Inlined rather than factored into a helper on purpose: this function is lifted out by name and

 1658:      * lift is a ReferenceError in the gate rather than a failure in the code.
 1659:      */

 1661:     for (var pi = 0; pi < paras.length; pi++) {
 1662:       var prs = paras[pi] && Array.isArray(paras[pi].runs) ? paras[pi].runs : [];
 1663:       for (var ri = 0; ri < prs.length; ri++) {
 1664:         var r = prs[ri];
 1665:         if (!r || typeof r !== 'object') continue;
 1666:         nRuns += 1;
 1667:         if (r.bold) nBold += 1;
 1668:         if (r.italic) nItalic += 1;
 1669:       }
 1670:     }
 1671: 
 1672:     return {
 1673:       id: e.id, type: e.type,
 1674:       x: e.x, y: e.y, w: e.w, h: e.h,
 1675:       // Absent or null rotation is 0. A rotation of "45" is NOT 45: it stays a string, and the judge

 1688:       bold: nRuns > 0 && nBold === nRuns,
 1689:       italic: nRuns > 0 && nItalic === nRuns,

 1693:   /* THE SECOND CALLER OF THE JUDGE, and the reason the judge exists as its own function.
 1694:    *
 1695:    * ALL OR NOTHING, and in this order: judge every element FIRST, touch the stage SECOND.
 1696:    *
 1697:    * A file is not a trusted input just because this editor may have written it -- it is a
 1698:    * hand-editable text file, and the moment one of its elements could come to rest on the logo,

 1700:    * against the mouse and not against a file.
