 1703:    * stage and overwrite the title before it had looked at a single element, so a per-element check
 1704:    * bolted into the loop -- however correct that check was -- could only ever leave a half-imported
 1705:    * stage behind and the user's own deck already gone. "Reject the entire import" is not reachable
 1706:    * from inside that loop. It is reachable from here.
 1707:    */
 1708:   function loadIR(ir) {
 1709:     if (!ir || typeof ir !== 'object') throw new Error('that file does not contain a deck');
 1710:     if (ir.schema !== SCHEMA) throw new Error('schema is ' + ir.schema + ', expected ' + SCHEMA);
 1711:     if (!ir.canvas || ir.canvas.w !== CANVAS.w || ir.canvas.h !== CANVAS.h) {
 1712:       throw new Error('that deck was authored in a different model space; it is never rescaled');
 1713:     }
 1714:     var raw = ir.elements === undefined || ir.elements === null ? [] : ir.elements;
 1715:     if (!Array.isArray(raw)) throw new Error('elements is not a list');
 1716: 
 1717:     /* PASS 1 -- JUDGE. Nothing below this line has touched the stage, and nothing above it may. */
 1718:     var states = [];
 1719:     var problems = [];
 1720:     var seen = Object.create(null); // no prototype, so an id of "__proto__" is just a key
 1721:     for (var i = 0; i < raw.length; i++) {
 1722:       var s = stateFromIR(raw[i]);
 1723:       states.push(s);
 1724: 
 1725:       // The same label validate.py prints, so the editor's refusal and the backend's 422 read as
 1726:       // one voice rather than as two tools that happen to disagree with your file.
 1727:       var at = 'elements[' + i + '] (id "' + (typeof s.id === 'string' ? s.id : '?') +
 1728:                '", type "' + (typeof s.type === 'string' ? s.type : '?') + '")';
 1729: 
 1730:       var why = illegalReason(s);
 1731:       if (why) problems.push(at + ': ' + why);
 1732: 
 1733:       // Ids become shape NAMES in the .pptx and the round-trip suite finds shapes by name, so two
 1734:       // elements sharing one makes one of them unfindable. validate.py rejects it; so do we.
 1735:       if (typeof s.id !== 'string' || !s.id) {
 1736:         problems.push(at + ': has no id -- an id becomes the shape name in the .pptx');
 1737:       } else if (s.id in seen) {
 1738:         problems.push(at + ': duplicate id -- already used by elements[' + seen[s.id] + ']');
 1739:       } else {
 1740:         seen[s.id] = i;
 1741:       }
 1742:     }
 1743: 
 1744:     if (problems.length) {
 1745:       throw new Error(
 1746:         problems.length + ' element(s) in that file may not be placed on this stage, so NOTHING ' +
 1747:         'was loaded and the deck you had is untouched:\n' +
 1748:         problems.map(function (p, n) { return '  ' + (n + 1) + '. ' + p; }).join('\n')
 1749:       );
 1750:     }
 1751: 
 1752:     /* PASS 2 -- COMMIT. Every state is legal, so this cannot fail half-way through. */
 1753:     [].slice.call(stage.querySelectorAll(':scope > .el')).forEach(function (n) { n.remove(); });
 1754:     select(null);
 1755:     titleField.textContent = ir.title || '';
 1756: 
 1757:     for (var j = 0; j < states.length; j++) {
 1758:       var el = makeEl(states[j]);
 1759:       stage.appendChild(el); // array order IS z-order -- preserve the file's order exactly
 1760:       var body = el.querySelector('.txt .body');
 1761:       var txt = raw[j] && typeof raw[j].text === 'object' ? raw[j].text : null;
 1762:       if (body && txt) body.innerHTML = paragraphsToHTML(txt.paragraphs, states[j].color);
 1763:     }
 1764:     setStatus('Loaded ' + states.length + ' element(s).');
 1765:   }
 1766: 
 1767:   /* Runs are hand-editable too. A paragraph with no `runs`, or a run with no `t`, is a file someone
 1768:    * edited -- not a file we wrote -- and it must not be a TypeError. It used to be one, and it was
 1769:    * thrown AFTER the stage had already been cleared.
 1770:    */
 1771:   function paragraphsToHTML(paragraphs, defColor) {
 1772:     var list = Array.isArray(paragraphs) ? paragraphs : [];
 1773:     return list.map(function (p) {
 1774:       var runs = p && Array.isArray(p.runs) ? p.runs : [];
 1775:       var inner = runs.map(function (r) {
 1776:         if (!r || typeof r !== 'object') return '';
 1777:         var raw = r.t === undefined || r.t === null ? '' : r.t;
 1778:         var t = esc(raw).replace(/\n/g, '<br>');
 1779:         if (r.bold) t = '<b>' + t + '</b>';
 1780:         if (r.italic) t = '<i>' + t + '</i>';
 1781:         // A run with no colour inherits the element's, and it does so EXPLICITLY: normalizeText()
 1782:         // reads the colour back off this span on the way out, so leaving it empty would round-trip
 1783:         // the colour as the string "undefined" and lose it.
 1784:         return '<span style="color: ' + esc(r.color || defColor) + '">' + t + '</span>';
 1785:       }).join('');
 1786:       return '<div>' + inner + '</div>';
 1787:     }).join('');
 1788:   }

  339:           // A resize or a drag-out: snap only the edges that actually moved. Snapping x during an
  340:           // SE-handle resize would slide the element instead of resizing it.
  341:           var x1 = c.x, x2 = c.x + c.w, y1 = c.y, y2 = c.y + c.h;
  342:           if (opt.edges.left) x1 = snapVal(x1, GUIDES_X);
  343:           if (opt.edges.right) x2 = snapVal(x2, GUIDES_X);
  344:           if (opt.edges.top) y1 = snapVal(y1, GUIDES_Y);
  345:           if (opt.edges.bottom) y2 = snapVal(y2, GUIDES_Y);
  346:           c.x = x1; c.w = x2 - x1; c.y = y1; c.h = y2 - y1;
  347:         } else {
  348:           c.x = snapAxis(c.x, c.w, GUIDES_X);
  349:           c.y = snapAxis(c.y, c.h, GUIDES_Y);
  350:         }
  351:         // Snapping must never talk the box below the minimum. If it did, undo it on that axis --
  352:         // the invariant outranks the convenience.
  353:         if (c.w < MIN_EXTENT) { c.x = px; c.w = pw; }
  354:         if (c.h < MIN_EXTENT) { c.y = py; c.h = ph; }
  355:       }
  356: 
  357:       // 4. clamp the ROTATED footprint into the slide, by translating.
  358:       var b = elementBBox(c);
  359:       if (b[2] - b[0] > CANVAS.w + EPS || b[3] - b[1] > CANVAS.h + EPS) return null;
  360:       var sx = 0, sy = 0;
  361:       if (b[0] < 0) sx = -b[0]; else if (b[2] > CANVAS.w) sx = CANVAS.w - b[2];
  362:       if (b[1] < 0) sy = -b[1]; else if (b[3] > CANVAS.h) sy = CANVAS.h - b[3];
  363:       c.x += sx; c.y += sy;
  364:     }
  365: 
  366:     // 5 + 6. THE JUDGE -- slide bounds and the reserved regions, on the rotated AABB. The rule is
  367:     // not written out here: it is illegalReason(), the same function, the same numbers and the same
  368:     // half-open comparisons that a file has to answer to. A second copy of it here is exactly how
  369:     // the mouse and the importer would drift apart.
  370:     return illegalReason(c) ? null : c;
  371:   }
  372: 
  373:   /* [REDACTED] state <-> dataset */
  374: 
  375:   function read(el) {
  376:     var d = el.dataset;
  377:     var s = { id: d.id, type: d.type };
  378:     if (d.type === 'connector') {
  379:       s.x1 = +d.x1; s.y1 = +d.y1; s.x2 = +d.x2; s.y2 = +d.y2;
  380:       s.kind = d.kind; s.arrow = d.arrow; s.color = d.color; s.width = +d.width;
  381:     } else {
  382:       s.x = +d.x; s.y = +d.y; s.w = +d.w; s.h = +d.h; s.rot = +d.rot || 0;
  383:       s.fill = d.fill || '';
  384:       s.strokeColor = d.strokeColor || '';
  385:       s.strokeWidth = d.strokeWidth === undefined || d.strokeWidth === '' ? 1 : +d.strokeWidth;
  386:       s.size = +d.size; s.color = d.color; s.align = d.align; s.valign = d.valign;
  387:       // A dataset value is a STRING, so '' and '0' and 'false' are all truthy traps. Compare.
  388:       s.bold = d.bold === '1'; s.italic = d.italic === '1';
  389:     }
  390:     return s;
  391:   }
  392: 
  393:   function write(el, s) {
  394:     var d = el.dataset;
  395:     d.id = s.id; d.type = s.type;
  396:     if (s.type === 'connector') {
  397:       d.x1 = s.x1; d.y1 = s.y1; d.x2 = s.x2; d.y2 = s.y2;
  398:       d.kind = s.kind; d.arrow = s.arrow; d.color = s.color; d.width = s.width;
  399:     } else {
  400:       d.x = s.x; d.y = s.y; d.w = s.w; d.h = s.h; d.rot = s.rot || 0;
  401:       d.fill = s.fill; d.strokeColor = s.strokeColor; d.strokeWidth = s.strokeWidth;
  402:       d.size = s.size; d.color = s.color; d.align = s.align; d.valign = s.valign;
  403:       // '1' or '' -- style.css keys off [data-bold='1'], so "off" must not be the string 'false'.
  404:       d.bold = s.bold ? '1' : ''; d.italic = s.italic ? '1' : '';
  405:     }
  406:   }
  407: 
  408:   /* commit -- the ONE way state changes. Nothing writes dataset geometry except through here.
  409:    *
  410:    * Which is exactly why undo hangs off it: "every mutation is undoable" is only a checkable
  411:    * sentence because there is one door to hang the check on.
  412:    */
  413:   function commit(el, patch, opt) {
  414:     var cur = read(el);
  415:     for (var k in patch) cur[k] = patch[k];
  416:     var legal = applyConstraints(cur, opt);
  417:     if (!legal) { flashBlocked(el); return false; }
  418:     // BEFORE the write, and only when we already know the write will happen: a REFUSED frame must
  419:     // not cost an undo step, or dragging a box into the header would fill the stack with states the
  420:     // user cannot see the difference between. Inside a gesture the snapshot was taken once already,
  421:     // at beginTx() -- taking another one every pointermove frame is the one place this could get
  422:     // expensive.
  423:     var before = txOpen ? null : snapshot();
  424:     write(el, legal);
  425:     renderEl(el);
  426:     noteChange(before);
  427:     return true;
  428:   }
  429: 
  430:   function flashBlocked(el) {
  431:     // Restart the animation across a frame boundary rather than by poking a layout property --
  432:     // reading one back would measure the DOM, which is the whole thing we do not do.
  433:     el.classList.remove('blocked');
  434:     requestAnimationFrame(function () { el.classList.add('blocked'); });
  435:     setTimeout(function () { el.classList.remove('blocked'); }, 400);
  436:   }
  437: 
  438:   /* ================================================================================ undo / redo
  439:    *
  440:    * SNAPSHOT-BASED, and that is a decision, not a shortcut.
  441:    *
  442:    * The obvious alternative -- a stack of commands, each with an inverse -- needs an inverse for
  443:    * every mutation the editor has. Move and resize invert easily. Raise/lower reorder the DOM.
  444:    * Delete has to remember where in the z-order the element WAS. Load replaces the entire stage.
  445:    * Duplicate mints an id. Each of those is a separate inverse, and each inverse that is subtly
  446:    * wrong is a silent corruption that surfaces three undos later, somewhere else. A snapshot has
  447:    * exactly ONE inverse and every mutation shares it.
  448:    *
  449:    * A snapshot is JS STATE ONLY -- dataset (via read()), the contenteditable's innerHTML, and DOM
  450:    * ORDER, which IS the z-order. It measures nothing, and restoring one rebuilds the elements
  451:    * through makeEl(), i.e. back down the same one-way state -> CSS path every other write takes.
  452:    * Undo therefore cannot reintroduce a device pixel, which is the one way an undo stack could
  453:    * plausibly have broken the Prime Directive.
  454:    *
  455:    * WHAT IS NOT IN A SNAPSHOT: zoom and the current tool. They are VIEW, not document. Undoing a
  456:    * move and having the zoom jump under you is not undo, it is time travel.
  457:    *
  458:    * GESTURE GROUPING. A drag commits a frame per pointermove, so ungrouped, one drag across the
  459:    * stage would be a hundred undo steps and Ctrl+Z would be useless. beginTx() at the gesture's
  460:    * start takes ONE snapshot; the first successful commit inside the gesture pushes it; endTx() at
  461:    * pointerup throws it away if nothing was ever committed -- so a click that merely SELECTS costs
  462:    * no history at all, which is the case that would otherwise make the stack unreadable.
  463:    */
  464: 
  465:   var HIST_MAX = 100; // deep enough to be an undo stack, bounded so a long session cannot grow forever
  466: 
  467:   var past = [];
  468:   var future = [];
  469: 
  470:   var txOpen = false;   // a gesture is in flight
  471:   var txBefore = null;  // the stage as it was when that gesture began
  472:   var txPushed = false; // ...and whether that snapshot is already on the stack
  473: 
  474:   function snapshot() {
  475:     return {
  476:       title: titleField.innerHTML,
  477:       // :scope > .el -- the same query the serializer uses, so the snapshot sees exactly what the
  478:       // IR sees: no pseudo-element chrome, no title field, and the array order IS the z-order.
  479:       els: [].slice.call(stage.querySelectorAll(':scope > .el')).map(function (el) {
  480:         var body = el.querySelector('.txt .body');
  481:         return {
  482:           state: read(el),
  483:           html: body ? body.innerHTML : null, // the runs live in the DOM, so they are part of the state
  484:           sel: el === selected,
  485:         };
  486:       }),
  487:     };
  488:   }
  489: 
  490:   /* Returns TRUE when the stage now holds `snap`, and FALSE when it refused it -- and on false the
  491:    * stage is byte-for-byte as it was. The caller owns the history stacks and may only move `snap`
  492:    * off one of them once this has returned true; see undo() and redo(). */
  493:   function restore(snap) {
  494:     /* PASS 1 -- JUDGE. Nothing above this line has touched the DOM, and nothing below it may run if
  495:      * the snapshot is refused. stopEditing() is DOM mutation (it drops .editing and removes the
  496:      * contenteditable attribute) and it calls endTx(), so it belongs BELOW the preflight, not
  497:      * above: "before touching the DOM" means before stopEditing(), not merely before the clear. */
  498:     var why = unrestorableReason(snap);
  499:     if (why) {
  500:       setStatus('That history step was refused, so nothing changed: ' + why);
  501:       return false;
  502:     }
  503: 
  504:     /* PASS 2 -- COMMIT. Every record is legal, so this cannot fail half-way through. */
  505:     stopEditing();
  506:     [].slice.call(stage.querySelectorAll(':scope > .el')).forEach(function (n) { n.remove(); });
  507:     // The node `selected` pointed at has just been removed, so the identity check at the top of
  508:     // select() would short-circuit against a corpse. Clear it before anything can consult it.
  509:     selected = null;
  510:     titleField.innerHTML = snap.title;
  511: 
  512:     var toSelect = null;
  513:     for (var i = 0; i < snap.els.length; i++) {
  514:       var rec = snap.els[i];
  515:       var el = makeEl(rec.state); // NOT commit(): a restore must not be TRANSFORMED -- not
  516:       stage.appendChild(el);      // re-snapped, not clamped, not pushed back onto the stack it came
  517:                                   // from. It HAS been judged, by unrestorableReason(), above.
  518:       var body = el.querySelector('.txt .body');
  519:       if (body && rec.html !== null) body.innerHTML = rec.html;
  520:       if (rec.sel) toSelect = el;
  521:     }
  522:     select(toSelect);
  523:     syncProps(); // select(null) short-circuits when nothing was selected, so ask for this outright
  524:     return true;
  525:   }
  526: 
  527:   function pushUndo(before) {
  528:     past.push(before);
  529:     if (past.length > HIST_MAX) past.shift();
  530:     future.length = 0; // a fresh edit forks the timeline -- the redos are unreachable now
  531:     syncHistory();
  532:   }
  533: 
  534:   function beginTx() {
  535:     if (txOpen) return; // a gesture already owns the step; do not start a second one inside it
  536:     txOpen = true;
  537:     txBefore = snapshot();
  538:     txPushed = false;
  539:   }
  540: 
  541:   /* Call AFTER a mutation has succeeded, never before. `before` is the pre-mutation snapshot, and
  542:    * it is only needed for changes that happen OUTSIDE a gesture -- inside one, txBefore is it. */
  543:   function noteChange(before) {
  544:     if (txOpen) {
  545:       if (!txPushed) { pushUndo(txBefore); txPushed = true; }
  546:       return;
  547:     }
  548:     if (before) pushUndo(before);
  549:   }
  550: 
  551:   function endTx() { txOpen = false; txBefore = null; txPushed = false; }
  552: 
  553:   /* PEEK, THEN POP -- never `restore(past.pop())`.
  554:    *
  555:    * A pop written as the argument is evaluated BEFORE the callee runs, so the entry is off the stack
  556:    * before restore() has done anything with it. If the restore is then refused, or throws on a
  557:    * malformed record, the stage has been cleared AND the only copy of the state that would put it
  558:    * back has been destroyed: the user loses the deck and the undo step in one keystroke. So the
  559:    * entry comes off the stack once the stage actually holds it, and not one statement sooner.
  560:    *
  561:    * A refused entry therefore STAYS on the stack. That is the fail-closed half of the trade: Ctrl+Z
  562:    * declining to move is a bug report, whereas silently discarding the user's only copy of a state
  563:    * is a bug we never hear about because the evidence went with it.
  564:    */
  565:   function undo() {
  566:     if (!past.length) { setStatus('Nothing to undo.'); return; }
  567:     endTx(); // a gesture may not straddle an undo
  568:     var now = snapshot();
  569:     if (!restore(past[past.length - 1])) return; // refused: restore() said why, and nothing moved
  570:     past.pop();
  571:     future.push(now);
  572:     syncHistory();
  573:     setStatus('Undo ??' + past.length + ' step(s) left.');
  574:   }
  575: 
  576:   function redo() {
  577:     if (!future.length) { setStatus('Nothing to redo.'); return; }
  578:     endTx();
  579:     var now = snapshot();
  580:     if (!restore(future[future.length - 1])) return;
  581:     future.pop();
  582:     past.push(now);
  583:     syncHistory();
  584:     setStatus('Redo ??' + future.length + ' step(s) left.');
  585:   }
  586: 
  587:   function syncHistory() {
  588:     $('b-undo').disabled = !past.length;
  589:     $('b-redo').disabled = !future.length;
  590:   }

    2: # -*- coding: utf-8 -*-
    3: """GATE 1 -- the Prime Directive, machine-checked. Phase 1B.
    4: 
    5: Static source assertions over static/editor.js and static/interactions.js. No browser, no node, no
    6: DOM: this is a grep, and that is the point. It is the only thing standing between "we agreed on the
    7: rule" and "the rule held", and it costs milliseconds.
    8: 
    9: THE RULE
   10: --------
   11: Geometry lives in JS state and flows ONE WAY into CSS. It is never read back out of the DOM. A
   12: device coordinate becomes a model coordinate in exactly ONE place -- toModel() -- and everything
   13: else works in model units.
   14: 
   15: Break it and the editor develops a class of bug that only appears at zoom != 1, presents as a
   16: coordinate error, and disagrees with canvas/geometry.py -- at which point the user is looking at a
   17: legal-looking stage and an unexplainable 422.
   18: 
   19: WHY THESE ARE SUBSTRING CHECKS AND NOT AST CHECKS
   20: [REDACTED]
   21: Because a substring check cannot be argued with, needs no JS toolchain in a Python test suite, and
   22: fails loudly the moment someone reaches for the convenient thing. The cost is that the banned names
   23: must not appear in editor.js AT ALL -- not even in a comment explaining why they are banned. That
   24: is why editor.js's own header points HERE for the list rather than reciting it: this file is the
   25: spec, and it cannot drift away from itself.
   26: """
   27: 
   28: from __future__ import annotations
   29: 
   30: import re
   31: from pathlib import Path
   32:

   36: 
   37: EDITOR_PATH = STATIC / "editor.js"
   38: INTERACTIONS_PATH = STATIC / "interactions.js"
   39: 
   40: EDITOR = EDITOR_PATH.read_text(encoding="utf-8")
   41: INTERACTIONS = INTERACTIONS_PATH.read_text(encoding="utf-8")
   42: 
   43: # Every way a browser will hand you a MEASURED number. Each one silently reintroduces device pixels
   44: # into the model and breaks the moment zoom != 1.

   46:     "offsetWidth",

   57: 
   58: # interact.js's move event exposes a measured, device-pixel rect. Consuming it is the single most
   59: # common way this rule dies. (Our own engine never emits one -- see interactions.js -- but the ban
   60: # has to survive someone swapping the real library back in.)
   61: BANNED_RECT = [r"\bev\.rect\b", r"\bevent\.rect\b"]
   62: 
   63: # Canvas sizes that are NOT the model space. 1280x720 divides exactly too, but then every font size
   64: # needs a x0.75 factor -- which is precisely the bug 960x540 exists to delete.
   65: OTHER_CANVAS_SIZES = ["1920", "1080", "1280", "720", "1440", "810"]
   66: 
   67: 
   68: def _function_body(src: str, name: str) -> str:
   69:     """The text of `function <name>(...) { ... }`, braces balanced."""
   70:     start = src.index(f"function {name}(")
   71:     open_brace = src.index("{", start)
   72:     depth = 0
   73:     for i in range(open_brace, len(src)):
   74:         if src[i] == "{":
   75:             depth += 1
   76:         elif src[i] == "}":
   77:             depth -= 1
   78:             if depth == 0:
   79:                 return src[open_brace : i + 1]
   80:     raise AssertionError(f"unbalanced braces in {name}()")
   81:

    1: /* Canvas editor styling.

   97:    id selector WINS and a hidden label would still be `display: flex`. The attribute would be set,

  289: 
  290: .el .txt {
  291:   position: absolute;
  292:   /* PowerPoint's default text-frame insets: 0.1in L/R, 0.05in T/B = 7.2 / 3.6 logical units.
  293:      Hard-coded on both sides. This is the trap that reads like a coordinate bug. */
  294:   left: 7.2px; right: 7.2px; top: 3.6px; bottom: 3.6px;
  295:   box-sizing: border-box;
  296:   padding: 0; margin: 0; border: 0;
  297: 
  298:   font-family: 'Century Gothic', 'Malgun Gothic', sans-serif;  /* mirrors latin/ea split */
  299:   font-size: 14px;              /* == 14 pt in the model */
  300:   line-height: 1.2;             /* must equal renderer line_spacing */
  301:   white-space: pre-wrap;        /* word_wrap = True */
  302:   overflow-wrap: break-word;
  303:   word-break: keep-all;         /* Korean: break at spaces, not mid-word */
  304: 
  305:   display: flex;                /* autoshape default is anchor="ctr" + algn="ctr" */
  306:   align-items: center;          /* valign ctr  -> tf.vertical_anchor = MSO_ANCHOR.MIDDLE */
  307:   justify-content: center;      /* align  ctr  -> p.alignment = PP_ALIGN.CENTER */
  308:   text-align: center;
  309:   overflow: visible;            /* show overflow; do NOT hide the problem */
  310: 
  311:   outline: none;
  312: 
  313:   /* The text box covers almost the whole element, so if it swallowed pointer events the user could
  314:      never DRAG a textbox -- every grab would land a caret instead. Click selects and drags;
  315:      DOUBLE-click opens the text for editing (editor.js adds .editing). This is the conventional
  316:      bargain and it is the only one that leaves a no-fill textbox movable at all. */
  317:   pointer-events: none;
  318: }
  319: .el.editing .txt { pointer-events: auto; cursor: text; }
  320: 
  321: /* CONTRACT: run.bold / run.italic.
  322:  *
  323:  *     .el[data-bold='1']   <->  style.py: run.font.bold   = True   (every run in the element)
  324:  *     .el[data-italic='1'] <->  style.py: run.font.italic = True
  325:  *
  326:  * These are ELEMENT-level flags over a PER-RUN property: normalizeText() OR-s them into every run
  327:  * on the way out, because run.bold is the only place the IR can carry them. A <b> inside .body still
  328:  * bolds its own run by itself -- so these rules are the preview of the FLAG, not of the tags, and
  329:  * the two are additive exactly as they are in the serializer.
  330:  *
  331:  * The value is the string '1'. dataset writes strings, so a false flag is '' -- never 'false',
  332:  * which would be truthy on the way back in and match nothing on the way down here. */
  333: .el[data-bold='1'] .txt   { font-weight: 700; }
  334: .el[data-italic='1'] .txt { font-style: italic; }
  335: 
  336: /* valign / align are written from state onto these data-* hooks, never read back out of CSS. */
  337: .el .txt[data-valign='TOP']    { align-items: flex-start; }
  338: .el .txt[data-valign='MIDDLE'] { align-items: center; }
  339: .el .txt[data-valign='BOTTOM'] { align-items: flex-end; }
  340: .el .txt[data-align='LEFT']    { justify-content: flex-start; text-align: left; }
  341: .el .txt[data-align='CENTER']  { justify-content: center;     text-align: center; }
  342: .el .txt[data-align='RIGHT']   { justify-content: flex-end;   text-align: right; }
  343: 
  344: /* THE single flex item. The Sec 5.6 contract above puts `display: flex` on .txt, and its
  345:    align-items/justify-content mapping (valign/align) is only correct when there is exactly ONE
  346:    flex item. A contenteditable that holds several paragraphs produces several block children --
  347:    which, as direct flex items, would lay out SIDE BY SIDE in a row. So .txt is the anchor box and
  348:    .body is the contenteditable: one flex item, paragraphs stacking normally inside it, and the
  349:    contract preserved exactly as written. */
  350: .el .txt > .body { width: 100%; outline: none; }
  351: 
  352: .el svg { overflow: visible; position: absolute; inset: 0; }
  353: 
  354: /* A connector is a line: it has no text frame and its SVG must be free to draw its stroke and
  355:    arrowheads outside the endpoints' AABB. */
  356: .el[data-type='connector'] { overflow: visible; }

 1280:   });

 1282:   /* [REDACTED] run.bold / run.italic
 1283:    *
 1284:    * The two IR properties the Phase 1B panel never exposed. They are ELEMENT-level controls over a
 1285:    * PER-RUN property, and the asymmetry is the whole difficulty:
 1286:    *
 1287:    *   ON  -- set the flag. normalizeText() OR-s it into every run on the way out, and style.css
 1288:    *          shows it via [data-bold='1']. Nothing is written into the body.
 1289:    *   OFF -- clear the flag AND unwrap every <b>/<strong> (resp. <i>/<em>) inside the body.
 1290:    *
 1291:    * The unwrap is not tidying, it is the difference between working and not. run.bold comes from
 1292:    * those tags as well as from the flag -- paragraphsToHTML() writes them on import, and the
 1293:    * browser's own Ctrl+B and any paste will too -- so an "off" that merely stopped ADDING bold
 1294:    * would leave the text bold on screen and in the .pptx, with the button showing un-pressed. The
 1295:    * control would look broken because it would BE broken.
 1296:    *
 1297:    * The cost is honest and worth stating: turning bold off on an element whose runs were only
 1298:    * PARTLY bold flattens that distinction. "Make this element not bold" is what the button says, and
 1299:    * it is what the button does.
 1300:    */
 1301:   function unwrapTags(root, tags) {
 1302:     var node;
 1303:     // A DOM edit, not a DOM measurement. normalizeText() already reads this very subtree -- it is
 1304:     // where runs come from -- so the body is state, not geometry, and the Prime Directive is silent
 1305:     // on it. Nested tags terminate: the outermost match is removed each pass.
 1306:     while ((node = root.querySelector(tags.join(',')))) {
 1307:       var parent = node.parentNode;
 1308:       while (node.firstChild) parent.insertBefore(node.firstChild, node);
 1309:       parent.removeChild(node);
 1310:     }
 1311:     root.normalize(); // merge the text nodes the unwrap left adjacent
 1312:   }
 1313: 
 1314:   function setTextFlag(which, on) {
 1315:     if (!selected || isConnector()) return; // a connector carries no text at all (schema: no `text`)
 1316:     beginTx(); // the unwrap and the flag are ONE undo step, not two
 1317:     var body = selected.querySelector('.txt .body');
 1318:     if (!on && body) unwrapTags(body, which === 'bold' ? ['b', 'strong'] : ['i', 'em']);
 1319:     var patch = {};
 1320:     patch[which] = on;
 1321:     patchSelected(patch); // geometry is untouched, so the judge cannot refuse this
 1322:     endTx();
 1323:     syncProps();
 1324:   }

 1111:     };

  730: # ===============================================================================================
  731: #
  732: # Phase 2 relocated the property controls into a right-hand panel that appears on selection, made
  733: # `contenteditable` an attribute that comes and goes, added run.bold / run.italic, and built the
  734: # undo/redo stack Phase 1B deferred.
  735: #
  736: # Three of those four are cheap to get subtly, silently wrong, so each has a gate here:
  737: #
  738: #   - the panel   -- a control that is VISIBLE but patches nothing is the bug being fixed, and the
  739: #                    CSS specificity trap below (`#props label` beats `[hidden]`) reintroduces it
  740: #                    without touching a line of JS.
  741: #   - the text    -- `contenteditable` was permanent, and only `pointer-events: none` kept the caret
  742: #                    out. A class is not an attribute and editingText() consults the attribute.
  743: #   - bold/italic -- an ELEMENT-level control over a PER-RUN property. The round trip is where this
  744: #                    goes wrong: summarise the flag off the FIRST run and a deck with one bold word
  745: #                    re-exports with the whole sentence bold.
  746: #
  747: # Undo is checked structurally (every mutation pushes) rather than behaviourally: exercising it needs
  748: # a real DOM, and stubbing one convincingly enough to trust the result would be a bigger lie than the
  749: # grep. What CAN be checked without a browser is checked, and it is checked here.
  750: 
  751: INDEX_HTML = (PROJECT_ROOT / "src" / "aippt" / "canvas" / "static" / "index.html").read_text(encoding="utf-8")
  752: STYLE_CSS = (PROJECT_ROOT / "src" / "aippt" / "canvas" / "static" / "style.css").read_text(encoding="utf-8")
  753: 
  754: # Every styling property canvas-ir/1 actually HAS, and the control that drives it. If a control here
  755: # stops existing the panel has lost a property; if one appears that is not here, it is patching
  756: # something the schema cannot carry and validate.py will answer with a 422.

  776:     return INDEX_HTML[start : INDEX_HTML.index("</aside>", start)]

  788:     """A panel of controls that patch nothing is a panel that lies.
  789: 
  790:     In Phase 1B these same inputs sat in the left toolbar and were permanently visible: with an empty

  823: def [REDACTED]():
  824:     """THE CSS SPECIFICITY TRAP, and it is not a style nit -- it is the whole scoping mechanism.
  825: 
  826:     `[hidden]` is a UA rule at (0,0,1,0). `#props label` is (0,1,0,1) and sets `display: flex`, so
  827:     the ID SELECTOR WINS: a label carrying `hidden` would be announced as hidden, excluded from the
  828:     accessibility tree -- and still be painted, and still be clickable. syncProps()'s data-for
  829:     scoping would silently do nothing, and a connector would be offered a fill it cannot serialize.
  830:     """
  831:     assert "#props [hidden]" in STYLE_CSS, (
  832:         "style.css does not override `display` for hidden descendants of #props, so `#props label` "
  833:         "wins on specificity and the data-for scoping hides nothing."
  834:     )
  835:     assert "#props[hidden]" in STYLE_CSS, "the panel itself is never hidden by CSS"
  836:

  934: def [REDACTED]():
  935:     """A snapshot is JS STATE -- dataset, innerHTML, DOM order. Restoring one rebuilds the elements
  936:     down the same one-way state -> CSS path every other write takes, so undo structurally CANNOT
  937:     reintroduce a device pixel. (Gate 1 enforces the ban globally; this says WHERE it is kept.)"""
  938:     body = _js_function("restore")

  941: 
  942: 
  943: # --- undo is the THIRD door into the stage, and it is judged like the other two --------------------
  944: #
  945: # The test above says restore() rebuilds from state and never measures. That is necessary and it is
  946: # not sufficient: it passed happily while restore() was sending snapshot geometry straight through
  947: # makeEl()/appendChild() with no call to illegalReason() at all. What follows is the missing half.
  948: #
  949: # unrestorableReason() is lifted out of editor.js by name and executed in Node, exactly like loadIR()
  950: # above -- but with NO STUBS AT ALL. There is no `stage`, no `titleField`, no `makeEl` in that
  951: # harness, so if the preflight ever reaches for the DOM it is a ReferenceError and the gate says so.
  952: # "It touches no DOM" is thereby an assertion and not a hope, which is the whole reason the preflight
  953: # is allowed to run before the stage has been cleared.
  954: 
  955: _PREFLIGHT_FUNCS = [
  956:     "intersects",
  957:     "elementBBox",
  958:     "outOfBounds",
  959:     "geometryIsReadable",
  960:     "illegalReason",
  961:     "unrestorableReason",
  962: ]
  963: 
  964: _PREFLIGHT_MAIN = """
  965: var out = { threw: false, error: '', reason: null };
  966: try {
  967:   out.reason = unrestorableReason(JSON.parse(require('fs').readFileSync(0, 'utf8')));
  968: } catch (e) {
  969:   out.threw = true;
  970:   out.error = String((e && e.message) || e);
  971: }
  972: process.stdout.write(JSON.stringify(out));
  973: """
  974: 
  975: 
  976: def _preflight(snap) -> dict:
  977:     """Run the REAL unrestorableReason() over `snap`, with no DOM in scope."""
  978:     src = "\n\n".join(
  979:         ["'use strict';"]
  980:         + [_js_decl(n) for n in _DECLS]

 succeeded in 1054ms:
  980:         + [_js_decl(n) for n in _DECLS]
  981:         + [_js_function(n) for n in _PREFLIGHT_FUNCS]
  982:         + [_PREFLIGHT_MAIN]
  983:     )
  984:     with tempfile.TemporaryDirectory() as d:  # NOT under PROJECT_ROOT -- see _tree()
  985:         harness = Path(d) / "restore_gate.js"
  986:         harness.write_text(src, encoding="utf-8")
  987:         proc = subprocess.run(
  988:             [NODE, str(harness)],
  989:             input=json.dumps(snap),
  990:             capture_output=True,
  991:             text=True,
  992:             encoding="utf-8",
  993:             timeout=60,
  994:         )
  995:     assert proc.returncode == 0, (
  996:         f"the harness itself failed to run -- this is a broken TEST, not a failing gate:\n"
  997:         f"{proc.stderr}"
  998:     )
  999:     out = json.loads(proc.stdout)
 1000:     assert not out["threw"], (
 1001:         "unrestorableReason() threw when handed a snapshot. It is called BEFORE the stage is "
 1002:         f"cleared, so it may refuse, but it may never throw and it may never touch the DOM: "
 1003:         f"{out['error']}"
 1004:     )
 1005:     return out

 1011:     s.update(over)

 1081: def [REDACTED]():
 1082:     """ORDER IS THE FIX. A check bolted into the loop cannot produce "abort the restoration" -- by
 1083:     the time that loop runs, the user's deck is already gone. And stopEditing() MUTATES THE DOM (it
 1084:     drops .editing and removes contenteditable) and ends the transaction, so "before touching the
 1085:     DOM" means above stopEditing(), not merely above the clear."""
 1086:     body = _js_code("restore")
 1087:     judged = body.index("unrestorableReason(")
 1088:     for dom_touch in ("stopEditing()", "n.remove()", "titleField.innerHTML", "makeEl("):
 1089:         assert judged < body.index(dom_touch), (
 1090:             f"restore() reaches {dom_touch} before it has judged the snapshot, so a refusal can only "
 1091:             f"leave a half-restored stage behind and the user's own deck already gone"
 1092:         )

 1102: def [REDACTED]():
 1103:     """It is called before the stage is cleared, so it must be inert in both directions: it may not
 1104:     mutate the snapshot (snapping or clamping a restore would silently rewrite the user's own history
 1105:     under them -- the push-out we refused the mouse) and it may not touch the DOM."""

 1114: @pytest.mark.parametrize("fn,stack", [("undo", "past"), ("redo", "future")])
 1115: def [REDACTED](fn: str, stack: str):
 1116:     """`restore(past.pop())` evaluates the pop AS THE ARGUMENT -- the entry is off the stack before
 1117:     restore() has done anything. If the restore is then refused or throws, the stage is cleared AND

 1161: # --- run.bold / run.italic: the round trip [REDACTED]
 1162: 
 1163: _P2_DECLS = ["BRAND_RED", "DEFAULTS", "BLOCK_TAGS"]
 1164: _P2_FUNCS = ["stripC0", "hex2", "cssColorToHex", "colorOf", "normalizeText", "stateFromIR"]
 1165: 
 1166: # A DOM small enough to be obviously correct: normalizeText() reads nodeType, tagName, nodeValue,
 1167: # childNodes, lastChild, style.color and getAttribute('color'), and nothing else. It MEASURES
 1168: # nothing, which is exactly why a stub this thin is honest here and would not be for undo.
 1169: _P2_DOM = """
 1170: function T(t) { return { nodeType: 3, nodeValue: t }; }
 1171: function E(tag, kids) {
 1172:   var e = {
 1173:     nodeType: 1, tagName: tag, childNodes: kids || [], style: {},
 1174:     getAttribute: function () { return null; },
 1175:   };
 1176:   e.lastChild = e.childNodes.length ? e.childNodes[e.childNodes.length - 1] : null;
 1177:   return e;
 1178: }
 1179: """
 1180: 
 1181: 
 1182: def _run_p2(main: str) -> object:
 1183:     parts = ["'use strict';", _P2_DOM]
 1184:     parts += [_js_decl(n) for n in _P2_DECLS]
 1185:     parts += [_js_function(n) for n in _P2_FUNCS]
 1186:     parts.append(main)
 1187:     with tempfile.TemporaryDirectory() as d:  # NOT under PROJECT_ROOT -- see _tree()
 1188:         harness = Path(d) / "p2_gate.js"
 1189:         harness.write_text("\n\n".join(parts), encoding="utf-8")
 1190:         proc = subprocess.run(
 1191:             [NODE, str(harness)], capture_output=True, text=True, encoding="utf-8", timeout=60
 1192:         )
 1193:     assert proc.returncode == 0, (
 1194:         f"the harness itself failed to run -- this is a broken TEST, not a failing gate:\n"
 1195:         f"{proc.stderr}"
 1196:     )
 1197:     return json.loads(proc.stdout)

 1233: def _state_of(runs: list) -> dict:
 1234:     e = rect(text={"align": "CENTER", "valign": "MIDDLE", "paragraphs": [{"runs": runs}]})
 1235:     return _run_p2(
 1236:         f"process.stdout.write(JSON.stringify(stateFromIR({json.dumps(e)})));"

 1240: @needs_node
 1241: def [REDACTED]():
 1242:     """THE ROUND-TRIP TRAP, and the reason stateFromIR() counts instead of peeking.
 1243: 
 1244:     size and colour are read off the FIRST run. Reading bold the same way would be actively
 1245:     destructive: the flag is OR-ed back into EVERY run on the way out, so a deck with one bold word
 1246:     in a plain sentence would reload with the flag on and re-export with the WHOLE SENTENCE bold. A
 1247:     round trip must not change the deck.
 1248: 
 1249:     So mixed runs summarise to OFF, and their own <b>/<i> tags -- which paragraphsToHTML() writes --
 1250:     carry the per-run bold instead. Nothing is lost either way.
 1251:     """
 1252:     plain = {"t": "b", "size": 14, "color": "#FFFFFF"}
 1253:     bold = {"t": "a", "size": 14, "color": "#FFFFFF", "bold": True}
 1254: 
 1255:     assert _state_of([bold, dict(bold, t="c")])["bold"] is True, "every run bold -> flag ON"
 1256:     assert _state_of([bold, plain])["bold"] is False, (
 1257:         "the FIRST run is bold and the second is not. If the flag came back ON, re-exporting this "
 1258:         "deck would bold the plain run too -- the round trip would have rewritten the user's deck."
 1259:     )
 1260:     assert _state_of([plain, bold])["bold"] is False
 1261: 
 1262: 
 1263: @needs_node
 1264: def [REDACTED]():
 1265:     italic = {"t": "a", "size": 14, "color": "#FFFFFF", "italic": True}
 1266:     assert _state_of([italic])["italic"] is True
 1267:     assert _state_of([italic, {"t": "b", "size": 14, "color": "#FFFFFF"}])["italic"] is False

  707:   function makeEl(s) {
  708:     var el = document.createElement('div');
  709:     el.className = 'el';
  710:     var html = '<svg xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" preserveAspectRatio="none"></svg>';
  711: 
  712:     if (s.type === 'connector') {
  713:       html += handleHTML('p1') + handleHTML('p2');
  714:     } else {
  715:       // .txt is the flex/anchor box (the Sec 5.6 contract); .body is the text. One flex item, so
  716:       // multiple paragraphs stack instead of laying out side by side.
  717:       //
  718:       // NOTE THE ABSENT contenteditable. It is added by the dblclick handler and removed by
  719:       // stopEditing(). It used to be hard-coded here, with only `pointer-events: none` on .txt
  720:       // keeping the caret out -- which meant the element was, to the keyboard and to every
  721:       // assistive technology, permanently editable, and editingText() could not tell "the caret is
  722:       // in the text" from "the text merely exists".
  723:       html += '<div class="txt"><div class="body"></div></div>';
  724:       for (var i = 0; i < HANDLES.length; i++) html += handleHTML(HANDLES[i]);
  725:       html += handleHTML('rot');

  728:     write(el, s);
  729:     renderEl(el);

  733: 
  734:   // Z-ORDER IS DOM ORDER AND NOTHING ELSE. No `z` field, no CSS stacking index on .el, no sort key
  735:   // -- any of those would be a second source of truth and would desync. Raise/lower are pure
  736:   // insertBefore/append.
  737:   // New connectors go to the FRONT of the array -- drawn first, therefore BEHIND the boxes they
  738:   // join. Front of array == back of the slide.
  739:   function addEl(el) {
  740:     if (el.dataset.type === 'connector') {
  741:       var firstShape = stage.querySelector(':scope > .el:not([data-type="connector"])');
  742:       stage.insertBefore(el, firstShape); // null => append
  743:     } else {
  744:       stage.appendChild(el);
  745:     }
  746:     return el;
  747:   }

  749:   /* [REDACTED] interactions */
  750: 
  751:   function wire(el) {
  752:     // Drag the body. The text and the handles are excluded: a pointerdown in the text must land a
  753:     // caret, and one on a handle must resize.
  754:     // select() FIRST, then beginTx(): select() closes any open text session, and closing one ends
  755:     // its transaction. Opening ours before that would hand our snapshot straight to endTx().
  756:     Interactions.draggable(el, {
  757:       ignoreFrom: '.txt, .handle',
  758:       enabled: function () { return tool === 'select'; },
  759:       start: function () { select(el); beginTx(); },
  760:       end: endTx,
  761:       move: function (ctx) {
  762:         var s = read(el);
  763:         // Device px -> logical units. ALWAYS. This is the corollary of the Prime Directive.
  764:         var dx = ctx.dx / zoom, dy = ctx.dy / zoom;
  765:         if (s.type === 'connector') {
  766:           commit(el, { x1: s.x1 + dx, y1: s.y1 + dy, x2: s.x2 + dx, y2: s.y2 + dy },
  767:                  { alt: ctx.pointer.altKey });
  768:         } else {
  769:           commit(el, { x: s.x + dx, y: s.y + dy }, { alt: ctx.pointer.altKey });
  770:         }
  771:       },
  772:     });
  773: 
  774:     if (el.dataset.type === 'connector') {
  775:       ['p1', 'p2'].forEach(function (which) {
  776:         var h = el.querySelector('.handle[data-h="' + which + '"]');
  777:         Interactions.pointerDrag(h, {
  778:           start: function () { select(el); beginTx(); },
  779:           end: endTx,
  780:           move: function (ctx) {
  781:             // Endpoints drag individually. NO GLUING -- begin_connect()/end_connect() are out of
  782:             // Phase 1 (D-3).
  783:             var p = toModel(ctx.pointer);
  784:             var patch = which === 'p1' ? { x1: p.x, y1: p.y } : { x2: p.x, y2: p.y };
  785:             commit(el, patch, { alt: ctx.pointer.altKey });
  786:           },
  787:         });
  788:       });
  789:       return;
  790:     }
  791: 
  792:     HANDLES.forEach(function (h) {
  793:       var node = el.querySelector('.handle[data-h="' + h + '"]');
  794:       Interactions.resizable(node, h, {
  795:         start: function () { select(el); beginTx(); },
  796:         end: endTx,
  797:         move: function (ctx) {
  798:           var s = read(el);
  799:           // deltaRect is DEVICE PIXELS, exactly as interact.js reports it. Each field is divided
  800:           // by zoom, in the same expression, or the whole thing breaks the moment zoom != 1.
  801:           var nx = s.x + ctx.deltaRect.left / zoom;
  802:           var ny = s.y + ctx.deltaRect.top / zoom;
  803:           var nw = s.w + ctx.deltaRect.width / zoom;
  804:           var nh = s.h + ctx.deltaRect.height / zoom;
  805:           commit(el, { x: nx, y: ny, w: nw, h: nh },
  806:                  { alt: ctx.pointer.altKey, edges: ctx.edges });
  807:         },
  808:       });
  809:     });
  810: 
  811:     var rotHandle = el.querySelector('.handle[data-h="rot"]');
  812:     Interactions.pointerDrag(rotHandle, {
  813:       start: function () { select(el); beginTx(); },
  814:       end: endTx,
  815:       move: function (ctx) {
  816:         var s = read(el);
  817:         var p = toModel(ctx.pointer);
  818:         var cx = s.x + s.w / 2, cy = s.y + s.h / 2;
  819:         // +90 so that "handle straight up" reads as 0 degrees.
  820:         var deg = (Math.atan2(p.y - cy, p.x - cx) * 180) / Math.PI + 90;
  821:         if (ctx.pointer.shiftKey) deg = Math.round(deg / 15) * 15;
  822:         deg = ((deg + 180) % 360 + 360) % 360 - 180; // normalize to (-180, 180]
  823:         commit(el, { rot: round6(deg) }, { alt: ctx.pointer.altKey });
  824:         syncProps();
  825:       },
  826:     });
  827: 
  828:     el.addEventListener('pointerdown', function () { if (tool === 'select') select(el); });
  829: 
  830:     // Typing is a mutation like any other, and it is grouped like any other: the session opened by
  831:     // the dblclick below is ONE undo step, however many keystrokes it holds.
  832:     var textBody = el.querySelector('.txt .body');
  833:     if (textBody) textBody.addEventListener('input', function () { noteChange(); });
  834: 
  835:     // Click selects and drags; DOUBLE-click opens the text. Without this a no-fill textbox could
  836:     // never be dragged at all -- its .txt covers the whole element and would eat every grab.
  837:     el.addEventListener('dblclick', function (ev) {
  838:       if (tool !== 'select' || el.dataset.type === 'connector') return;
  839:       ev.preventDefault();
  840:       select(el);
  841:       var body = el.querySelector('.txt .body');
  842:       if (!body) return;
  843:       // THE ATTRIBUTE, not merely the class. `.el.editing .txt { pointer-events: auto }` decides
  844:       // whether a POINTER can reach the text; contenteditable decides whether the element is
  845:       // editable at all. Only the second one is what editingText() -- and the keyboard, and a screen
  846:       // reader -- actually consults.
  847:       body.setAttribute('contenteditable', 'true');
  848:       el.classList.add('editing');
  849:       beginTx(); // the whole editing session is one step; stopEditing() closes it
  850:       body.focus();
  851:     });
  852:   }
  853: 
  854:   function stopEditing() {
  855:     var open = stage.querySelectorAll('.el.editing');
  856:     for (var i = 0; i < open.length; i++) {
  857:       open[i].classList.remove('editing');
  858:       // Drop the attribute with the class. An element that is not being edited is not editable --
  859:       // leaving it behind is what made `contenteditable` permanent in the first place.
  860:       var body = open[i].querySelector('.txt .body');
  861:       if (body) body.removeAttribute('contenteditable');
  862:     }
  863:     // The session ends here. If nothing was typed, txPushed is false and the snapshot is simply
  864:     // dropped -- opening the text and closing it again is not an edit.
  865:     endTx();
  866:   }
